W:{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathit{op}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
mapping
0
to
{\mathbb{1}}
and
1
to
{\mathscr{V}}
.
13
13

13

Recall from Section
2.3
that
{\mathbb{1}}
is the unit
{\mathscr{V}}
-category, with only one object
0
and with
{\mathbb{1}}(0,0)=e
.
On arrows,
W\delta^{r}
sends the unique object of
{\mathbb{1}}
to
r\in{\mathscr{V}}_{o}

\textstyle{{\mathbb{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0\mapsto r}
\textstyle{\mathscr{V}}

Furthermore, let
F:{\mathbb{A}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
be the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor which maps the arrow
\delta^{r}:1\longrightarrow 0
to the “evaluation at
r
”
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor

\textstyle{DV({\mathscr{X}}^{\mathscr{V}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{DV{\mathscr{X}}}

that is,
F\delta^{r}(f)=f(r)
.
Now spelling out Definition
2.5
of what it would mean for
{\mathscr{X}}
to be the colimit
W*F
, we see that for each
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{Y}}
,

{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{Y}})\cong[{\mathbb{A}}^{\mathit{op}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}](W,{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(F-,{\mathscr{Y}}))

an object of the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category on the right hand side amounts to a map on objects
g:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
satisfying

[s,r]\leq{\mathscr{Y}}(g(f(s)),g(f(r)))

(19)

for all
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors
f:{\mathscr{V}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{X}}
. For
{\mathscr{X}}
to be a colimit, one needs the implication

r\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\ \Rightarrow r\leq{\mathscr{Y}}(g(x^{\prime}),g(x))

to hold for all
r\in{\mathscr{V}}
, which follows from (
19
), if one can find
f:{\mathscr{V}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{X}}
such that
f(e)=x^{\prime}
and
f(r)=x
. But note that, in the case of
{\mathscr{V}}=[0,\infty]
and
{\mathscr{X}}=\{x^{\prime},x\}
with
0<_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)<_{\mathbb{R}}\infty
no such
f
exists. Indeed, it would be interesting to define
{\mathscr{X}}
to be “path-connected”, or
{\mathscr{V}}
-connected, if for all
r\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)
there is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
f:{\mathscr{V}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{X}}
such that
f(e)=x^{\prime},f(r)=x
. ∎

Intuitively, what goes wrong with trying to set up (
18
) as a colimit is that
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors
f:{\mathscr{V}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{X}}
can be perceived as “paths” and that a general
{\mathscr{X}}
need not be “path-connected”. While this may be interesting to pursue in the future, the solution of our problem is to replace (
18
) by

\textstyle{DX_{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{r}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{r}_{1}}
\textstyle{DV{\mathscr{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\mathscr{X}}

(20)

where the sets
X_{r}
were introduced in (
5
) as
X_{r}=\{(x^{\prime},x)\mid r\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\}
and can equally be described as
V{\mathscr{X}}^{{\mathbb{2}}_{r}}
(see (
11
) and Example
3.4
) in total accordance now with (
17
).

Below we give the full details and prove that every
{\mathscr{X}}
is a colimit as in (
20
), from which the existence of left-Kan extensions along
D
then follows.

We use the letter “N” in various fonts and notations
to indicate the analogy with the nerve of an ordinary category.

{defi}

A
{\mathscr{V}}
-coinserter is a colimit that has the weight

N:\mathbb{N}^{\mathit{op}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}},

where
{\mathbb{N}}

is the
free

{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-category built upon the following ordinary category
\mathsf{N}
: the objects are all
r
in
{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
, together with an extra symbol
{{\sf o}}
,
with arrows

\textstyle{r\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{1}^{r}}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{0}^{r}}
\textstyle{{\sf o}}

for
r
ranging over the elements of
{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
.
The weight
N
is the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor sending
{{\sf o}}
to
{\mathbb{1}}
, and
r
to
{\mathbb{2}}_{r}
.
The action of
N
on arrows is defined as follows:

N\delta^{r}_{0}:{\mathbb{1}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{2}}_{r}
sends
0
to
0
, while
N\delta^{r}_{1}:{\mathbb{1}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{2}}_{r}
sends
0
to
1
:

\textstyle{\mathbb{2}_{r}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathbb{1}}
\scriptstyle{N\delta^{r}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{N\delta^{r}_{0}}

Remark 8
.

As
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is complete and cocomplete as a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-category itself
[
40
, Sections 3.2, 3.10]
,
{\mathscr{V}}
-coinserters do exist in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}

and can be computed using copowers and conical colimits, the latter of which, on the level of objects, are computed as in
{\mathsf{Set}}_{o}
because of the topologicity of the forgetful functor
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}_{o}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}_{o}

[
37
]
. But this general recipe is not so easy to use in practice, and we will provide in Corollary
13
and Lemma
30
alternatives for computing the
N
-weighted colimits of interest to us.
∎

We are now going to show that every
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-coinserter of discrete
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories which we would like to think of as components of “its
{\mathscr{V}}
-nerve”.

Proposition 9
.

Let
{\mathscr{X}}
be a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category and let
F\!_{\mathscr{X}}:{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
be given by

\displaystyle\mathsf{N}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}_{o}

\displaystyle{{\sf o}}

\displaystyle\ \mapsto\ X_{{\sf o}}\mbox{ , the set of objects of ${\mathscr{X}}$}

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle\ \mapsto\ X_{r}=\{(x^{\prime},x)\in X_{{\sf o}}\times X_{{\sf o}}\mid r\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\}

with
F\!_{\mathscr{X}}\delta^{r}_{0}
and
F\!_{\mathscr{X}}\delta^{r}_{1}
the evident projections.
Then the colimit

{N}\mathop{*}{(DF_{\mathscr{X}})}

of the diagram

(N:\mathbb{N}^{\mathit{op}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}},DF_{\mathscr{X}}:{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}})

in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is isomorphic to
{\mathscr{X}}
.

Proof 3.1
.

The colimit
{N}\mathop{*}{(DF\!_{\mathscr{X}})}
exists in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
, since
the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-category
{\mathbb{N}}
is small.

To ease the notation, we put

\displaystyle{\mathscr{X}}_{{\sf o}}=DF_{\mathscr{X}}({{\sf o}})\quad\quad\quad\quad{\mathscr{X}}_{r}=DF_{\mathscr{X}}(r)

\displaystyle\ \ \partial^{r}_{0}=DF_{\mathscr{X}}\delta^{r}_{0}\quad\quad\quad\quad\ \partial^{r}_{1}=DF_{\mathscr{X}}\delta^{r}_{1}

Let us analyse the defining isomorphism

{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}({N}\mathop{*}{(DF_{\mathscr{X}})},{\mathscr{Y}})\cong[{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathit{op}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}](N,{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DF_{\mathscr{X}}{-},{\mathscr{Y}}))

of
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories, natural in
{\mathscr{Y}}
.
The
{\mathscr{V}}
-category

[{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathit{op}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}](N,{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DF_{\mathscr{X}}{-},{\mathscr{Y}}))

of
N
-weighted “cocones” for
DF_{\mathscr{X}}
is described explicitly as follows.

(1)

The objects are
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformations

\tau:N\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DF_{\mathscr{X}}{-},{\mathscr{Y}}).

Each such
\tau
consists of the following
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors:

•

\tau_{{\sf o}}:N{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\sf o}},{\mathscr{Y}})
.

Since
N{{\sf o}}={\mathbb{1}}
,
\tau_{{\sf o}}

picks up a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor

f_{{\sf o}}:{\mathscr{X}}_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}.

No other restrictions are imposed since
{\mathbb{1}}(0,0)=e
.

•

\tau_{r}:Nr\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}({\mathscr{X}}_{r},{\mathscr{Y}})
.

This
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor picks up
two
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors

f^{r}_{0}:{\mathscr{X}}_{r}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}

and

f^{r}_{1}:{\mathscr{X}}_{r}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}.

Since
{\mathscr{X}}_{r}
is discrete, both
f^{r}_{0}
and
f^{r}_{1}
are
defined by their object-assignments only. There is, however,
the constraint below, because
Nr={\mathbb{2}}_{r}
:

r\leq\bigwedge_{r\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)}{\mathscr{Y}}(f^{r}_{0}(x^{\prime},x),f^{r}_{1}(x^{\prime},x))

In addition to the above, there are various commutativity conditions since
\tau

is natural. Explicitly, for
\delta^{r}_{0}:r\longrightarrow{{\sf o}}
, we have the commutative square

\textstyle{N{{\sf o}}={\mathbb{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{{\sf o}}}
\scriptstyle{N\delta^{r}_{0}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}({\mathscr{X}}_{{\sf o}},{\mathscr{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(\partial^{r}_{0},{\mathscr{Y}})}
\textstyle{Nr={\mathbb{2}}_{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{r}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}({\mathscr{X}}_{r},{\mathscr{Y}})}

that, on the level of objects, is the requirement

f_{{\sf o}}\cdot\partial^{r}_{0}=f^{r}_{0}

Similarly, for
\delta^{r}_{1}:r\longrightarrow{{\sf o}}
, we obtain

f_{{\sf o}}\cdot\partial^{r}_{1}=f^{r}_{1}

We conclude that to give
\tau:N\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DF_{\mathscr{X}}{-},{\mathscr{Y}})
reduces to a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor

f_{{\sf o}}:{\mathscr{X}}_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}

(and, recall, this
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor is given just by the
object-assignment
x\mapsto f_{{\sf o}}x
, since
{\mathscr{X}}_{{\sf o}}
is discrete) such that

r\leq{\mathscr{Y}}(f_{{\sf o}}x^{\prime},f_{{\sf o}}x)

holds for every object
(x^{\prime},x)
in

{\mathscr{X}}_{r}
and every
r
,
which means precisely that

{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\leq{\mathscr{Y}}(f_{{\sf o}}x^{\prime},f_{{\sf o}}x)

holds.

(2)

Given
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformations
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
, then
the
{\mathscr{V}}
-distance between them is given by

[{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathit{op}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}](N,{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DF_{\mathscr{X}}{-},{\mathscr{Y}}))(\tau,\tau^{\prime})=\bigwedge_{x}{\mathscr{Y}}(f_{{\sf o}}x,f^{\prime}_{{\sf o}}x)

where
f_{{\sf o}}
corresponds to
\tau
and
f^{\prime}_{{\sf o}}
corresponds to
\tau^{\prime}
.

From the above description of the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category of
N
-weighted “cocones” for
DF_{\mathscr{X}}
, it follows that the
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor

q_{\mathscr{X}}:{\mathscr{X}}_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{X}}

(21)

that sends each object
x
to itself is the couniversal such “cocone”.
More precisely, the inequality
r\leq{\mathscr{X}}(q_{\mathscr{X}}x^{\prime},q_{\mathscr{X}}x)
holds for every
(x^{\prime},x)
in
{\mathscr{X}}_{r}
and every
r
.

Furthermore, given any
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
f_{{\sf o}}:{\mathscr{X}}_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
with the above
properties, there is a unique
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor

f_{{\sf o}}^{\sharp}:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
such that
f_{{\sf o}}^{\sharp}q_{\mathscr{X}}=f_{{\sf o}}
holds.

The “2-dimensional aspect” of the colimit, that is,

\bigwedge_{x}{\mathscr{Y}}(f^{\sharp}_{{\sf o}}x,f^{\prime\sharp}_{{\sf o}}x)=\bigwedge_{x}{\mathscr{Y}}(f_{{\sf o}}x,f^{\prime}_{{\sf o}}x).

is satisfied because
f^{\sharp}_{{\sf o}}
and
f_{{\sf o}}
coincide on objects.
Hence we have proved that
{\mathscr{X}}
is isomorphic to
{N}\mathop{*}{(DF_{\mathscr{X}})}
.

Remark 10
.

(1)

Assume that the quantale
{\mathscr{V}}
is integral. Then following the same steps as in the proof above, one can see that the colimit
N\ast F_{DX}
exists in
{\mathsf{Set}}
and equals
X
.
Intuitively, what is happening is that if
e=\top
, the diagram is non-empty everywhere and
D
is fully faithful on non-empty domains, so it reflects colimits in general and the colimit
DX=N\ast DF_{DX}
in particular.

(2)

The ordinary category
\mathsf{N}
carries information about the elements of
{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
, but not about its order. This may seem peculiar at first sight, but the order is actually captured within the observation that the identity-on-objects
{\mathsf{Id}}_{r,s}:{\mathbb{2}}_{r}=N(r)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{2}}_{s}=N(s)
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor iff
r\leq s
, and it is the only
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor such that
{\mathsf{Id}}_{r,s}\circ N\delta^{r}_{i}=N\delta^{s}_{i}
,
i=0,1
.
Hence nothing is gained if additionally
\mathbb{N}
is enhanced with arrows witnessing the order relation in
{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
, compatible with the existing arrows
r\rightrightarrows{{\sf o}}
and with (the transitivity of) the order relation in
{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
.

The lemma above allows us to compute left Kan extensions along
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
:

Theorem 11
.

Every functor
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}

has a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-enriched left Kan extension
H^{\sharp}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}

along
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
given by
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}={N}\mathop{*}{(HF\!_{\mathscr{X}})}
.

Proof 3.2
.

Based on Proposition
9
, we will prove the following.
If we define
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
as the colimit

{N}\mathop{*}{(HF\!_{\mathscr{X}})}
, then
the assignment
{\mathscr{X}}\mapsto H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
can be extended to a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
that is a left Kan extension of
H
along
D
.

Suppose
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is given. We will use the notation employed in the proof of Proposition
9
.

(1)

We first define a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
H^{\sharp}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.

For every small
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
, let
F_{\mathscr{X}}
be as in Proposition
9
. Recall that for every
r\in{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
, we put
F_{\mathscr{X}}(r)=X_{r}
, the set of pairs
(x^{\prime},x)

such that
r\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)
, and
F_{\mathscr{X}}({{\sf o}})=X_{{\sf o}}
, the set of objects of
{\mathscr{X}}
.
Analogously, for a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
f:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
, we now denote by

f_{r}:X_{r}\longrightarrow Y_{r}
and
f_{{\sf o}}:X_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow Y_{{\sf o}}
the maps corresponding to
(x^{\prime},x)\mapsto(fx^{\prime},fx)
and the object assignment of
f
, respectively.
Let also put
d^{r}_{0}=F_{\mathscr{X}}\delta^{r}_{0}
and
d^{r}_{1}=F_{\mathscr{X}}\delta^{r}_{1}
.

We define

H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}={N}\mathop{*}{(HF_{\mathscr{X}})},

(22)

where
N
is as in Definition
3.2
.

Unravelling the definition of the weighted colimit, the
1
-dimensional aspect says that to give a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
f^{\sharp}:H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
is the same as to give a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
f:HX_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
such that

r\leq\bigwedge_{C\in HX_{r}}{\mathscr{Y}}(fHd^{r}_{0}(C),fHd^{r}_{1}(C))

(23)

holds for all
r
.
14
14

14

By slight abuse of language, we will use here and subsequently notation like
C\in HX_{r}
to mean that
C
runs through all objects in
the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category

HX_{r}
.

In particular, there is a “quotient”
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
c_{\mathscr{X}}:HX_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
such that

r\leq\bigwedge_{C\in HX_{r}}H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}(c_{\mathscr{X}}Hd^{r}_{0}(C),c_{\mathscr{X}}Hd^{r}_{1}(C))

(24)

holds for all
r
, with the property that any
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
HX_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
satisfying (
23
) uniquely factorizes through
c_{\mathscr{X}}
.

The 2-dimensional aspect of the colimit says that given any
f,g:HX_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
satisfying (
23
), the relation

\bigwedge_{B\in HX_{{\sf o}}}{\mathscr{Y}}(f(B),g(B))=\bigwedge_{A\in H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}}{\mathscr{Y}}(f^{\sharp}(A),g^{\sharp}(A))

(25)

holds.

For a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
f:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
we recall that the diagram

\textstyle{X_{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{1}^{r}}
\scriptstyle{d_{0}^{r}}
\scriptstyle{f_{r}}
\textstyle{X_{{\sf o}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{{\sf o}}}
\textstyle{Y_{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{1}^{r}}
\scriptstyle{d_{0}^{r}}
\textstyle{Y_{{\sf o}}}

commutes serially. Hence
f
induces a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation

F_{f}:F_{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow F_{\mathscr{Y}}
. Therefore we can define

H^{\sharp}f:H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{Y}}

as the unique mediating
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor

{N}\mathop{*}{(HF_{f})}:{N}\mathop{*}{(HF_{\mathscr{X}})}\longrightarrow{N}\mathop{*}{(HF_{\mathscr{Y}})}

In particular, we have the commutative diagram below:

\textstyle{HX_{{\sf o}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\mathscr{X}}}
\scriptstyle{Hf_{{\sf o}}}
\textstyle{H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H^{\sharp}f}
\textstyle{HY_{{\sf o}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{\mathscr{Y}}}
\textstyle{H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{Y}}}

Also, from the 2-dimensional aspect of the colimit (see Eq. (
25
)), we have that for any
f,g:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
, the equality below holds:

\bigwedge_{B\in HX_{{\sf o}}}H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{Y}}(c_{\mathscr{Y}}Hf_{{\sf o}}(B),c_{\mathscr{Y}}Hg_{{\sf o}}(B))=\bigwedge_{A\in H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}}H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{Y}}(H^{\sharp}f(A),H^{\sharp}g(A))

(26)

It remains to prove that the inequality

{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{Y}})(f,g)\leq{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}},H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{Y}})(H^{\sharp}f,H^{\sharp}g)

is satisfied.
To that end, suppose that
r\leq{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{Y}})(f,g)

holds. This is equivalent to the fact that there is a
mapping

t:X_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow Y_{r}
such that the triangles

\textstyle{X_{{\sf o}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{f_{{\sf o}}}
\textstyle{Y_{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{0}^{r}}
\textstyle{X_{{\sf o}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{g_{{\sf o}}}
\textstyle{Y_{r}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d_{1}^{r}}
\textstyle{Y_{{\sf o}}}
\textstyle{Y_{{\sf o}}}

(27)

commute. In fact,
t(x)=(f(x),g(x))
.
To prove that

r\leq{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}},H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{Y}})(H^{\sharp}f,H^{\sharp}g)

holds, we need to prove the inequality

r\leq\bigwedge_{A\in H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}}H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{Y}}(H^{\sharp}f(A),H^{\sharp}g(A))

This follows from:

\displaystyle r\leq

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{C\in HY_{r}}H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{Y}}(c_{\mathscr{Y}}Hd^{r}_{0}(C),c_{\mathscr{Y}}Hd^{r}_{1}(C))

by (
24
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{B\in HX_{{\sf o}}}H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{Y}}(c_{\mathscr{Y}}Hd^{r}_{0}Ht(B),c_{\mathscr{Y}}Hd^{r}_{1}Ht(B))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{B\in HX_{{\sf o}}}H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{Y}}(c_{\mathscr{Y}}Hf_{{\sf o}}(B),c_{\mathscr{Y}}Hg_{{\sf o}}(B))

by (
27
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{A\in H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}}H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{Y}}(H^{\sharp}f(A),H^{\sharp}g(A))

by (
26
)

Preservation of composition and identity follows easily from the colimit property, hence we obtain that the correspondence
{\mathscr{X}}\mapsto H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
can be extended to a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
H^{\sharp}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.

(2)

We show now that
H^{\sharp}\cong{\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}H
holds. As explained in
[
40
, Section 4.3]
(see also Remark
6
(
2
)), it is enough to check that for any
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
K:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
, there is a one-to-one correspondence between
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformations
H^{\sharp}\longrightarrow K
and
H\longrightarrow KD
.

Due to the definition of
H^{\sharp}
, there is a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation
\alpha:H\longrightarrow H^{\sharp}D
having as components the
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors
\alpha_{X}=c_{DX}
.
We will prove that
\alpha
is the unit of a left Kan extension.

Let
\tau:H^{\sharp}\longrightarrow K
be a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation.
Then the composite

\textstyle{H\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{H^{\sharp}D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau D}
\textstyle{KD}

is a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation
\tau^{\flat}:H\longrightarrow KD
.

Conversely, let
\sigma:H\longrightarrow KD
be a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation.
To give a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation
\sigma^{\sharp}:H^{\sharp}\longrightarrow K
means to give a family of
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors

\sigma^{\sharp}:H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{K}}{\mathscr{X}}

satisfying the naturality condition (
12
).
In turn, each
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
\sigma^{\sharp}:H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{K}}{\mathscr{X}}
is uniquely determined by a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
HX_{o}\longrightarrow K{\mathscr{X}}
such that (
23
) holds.

For this, we choose the composite
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor

HX_{o}\overset{\sigma_{X_{o}}}{\longrightarrow}KDX_{o}\overset{Kq_{{\mathscr{X}}}}{\longrightarrow}K{\mathscr{X}}

where
q_{\mathscr{X}}:DX_{o}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{X}}
is the canonical cocone (
21
) associated to each
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
.

We verify (
23
) for each
r\in{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
:

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ \ \bigwedge_{(x^{\prime},x)\in X_{r}}{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{(x^{\prime},x)\in\mathsf{ob}DX_{r}}{\mathscr{X}}(q_{{\mathscr{X}}}d^{r}_{0}(x^{\prime},x),q_{{\mathscr{X}}}d^{r}_{1}(x^{\prime},x))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[DX_{r},{\mathscr{X}}](q_{{\mathscr{X}}}d^{r}_{0},q_{{\mathscr{X}}}d^{r}_{1})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle[KDX_{r},K{\mathscr{X}}](K(q_{\mathscr{X}}d^{r}_{0}),K(q_{\mathscr{X}}d^{r}_{1}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{B\in\mathsf{ob}KDX_{r}}K{\mathscr{X}}(Kq_{{\mathscr{X}}}Kd^{r}_{0}(B),Kq_{{\mathscr{X}}}Kd^{r}_{1}(B))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{C\in\mathsf{ob}HX_{r}}K{\mathscr{X}}(Kq_{{\mathscr{X}}}Kd^{r}_{0}\sigma_{X_{r}}(C),Kq_{{\mathscr{X}}}Kd^{r}_{1}\sigma_{X_{r}}(C))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{C\in\mathsf{ob}HX_{r}}K{\mathscr{X}}(Kq_{{\mathscr{X}}}\sigma_{X_{o}}Hd^{r}_{0}(C),Kq_{{\mathscr{X}}}\sigma_{X_{o}}Hd^{r}_{1}(C))

In the above, we have used that
K
is a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor and the naturality of
\sigma
.

Hence there is a unique
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
\sigma^{\sharp}_{\mathscr{X}}:H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow K{\mathscr{X}}
such that
\sigma^{\sharp}_{\mathscr{X}}c_{\mathscr{X}}=Kq_{\mathscr{X}}\sigma_{X_{o}}
holds.
Recall that
c_{\mathscr{X}}:HX_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
is the colimiting cocone.

We leave to the reader the verifications that the
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors
\sigma_{\mathscr{X}}
are the components of a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation
\sigma^{\sharp}:H^{\sharp}\longrightarrow K
, and that the correspondences
\tau\mapsto\tau^{\flat}
and

\sigma\mapsto\sigma^{\sharp}
are inverses to each other.

Corollary 12
.

D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is dense.

Proof 3.3
.

It follows from Proposition
9
and Theorem
11
that the identity
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is the left Kan extension of
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
along
D
. But this is one of the equivalent definitions of density in
[
40
, Thm.5.1]

To be able to compute these left Kan extensions in concrete examples, we show that from the proof above one can extract a more explicit construction in terms of ‘‘shortest paths’’.
15
15

15

Left Kan extensions can be computed pointwise as colimits, hence using copowers and conical colimits. Due to the topologicity of
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}_{0}
over
{\mathsf{Set}}_{0}
, the latter are obtained (on object-level) as in
{\mathsf{Set}}_{0}
and endowed with the corresponding
{\mathscr{V}}
-metric structure. This is why the subsequent formula (
28
) is reminiscent of the usual construction of quotient metric spaces
[
59
]
.

Corollary 13
.

The left Kan extension
H^{\sharp}={\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}H
of a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is obtained as follows:

(1)

For a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
,
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
is the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category having the same objects as
HX_{{\sf o}}
(that is, the underlying set of objects of the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category obtained by applying
H
to
X_{{\sf o}}
, the set of objects of
{\mathscr{X}}
).
For any objects
A^{\prime}
and
A
of
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
, their corresponding
{\mathscr{V}}
-hom
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)
is given by

\begin{gathered}H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)=\bigvee\{{HX_{{\sf o}}(A^{\prime}_{0},A_{0})}{\otimes}r_{1}{\otimes}HX_{{\sf o}}(A^{\prime}_{1},A_{1}){\otimes}{\ldots}{\otimes}r_{n}{\otimes}HX_{{\sf o}}(A_{n}^{\prime},A_{n})\}\end{gathered}

(28)

where the suprema is computed over all paths

(A^{\prime}_{0},A_{0}),(A^{\prime}_{1},A_{1}),\ldots,(A^{\prime}_{n},A_{n})

where
A^{\prime}=A^{\prime}_{0}
,
A=A_{n}
, and all (possibly empty) tuples of elements

(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n})

such that there are objects
C_{i}
in
HX_{r_{i}}
with
Hd^{r_{i}}_{0}(C_{i})=A_{i-1}
,
Hd^{r_{i}}_{1}(C_{i})=A^{\prime}_{i}
, for all
i=1,n
:

\textstyle{C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Hd^{r_{1}}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{Hd^{r_{1}}_{1}}
\textstyle{C_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Hd^{r_{2}}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{Hd^{r_{2}}_{1}}

\scriptstyle{.\ .\ .}
\textstyle{\quad C_{n}\,\,\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Hd^{r_{n}}_{1}}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}=A_{0}^{\prime},A_{0}}
\textstyle{{\quad A^{\prime}_{1},A_{1}\quad}}
\textstyle{{\quad A^{\prime}_{2},A_{2}\quad}}
\textstyle{{\quad A^{\prime}_{n-1},A_{n-1}\quad}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Hd^{r_{n}}_{0}}
\textstyle{A_{n}^{\prime},A_{n}=A}

(2)

The couniversal cocone
c_{\mathscr{X}}:HX_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
is the identity on objects.

(3)

For a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
f:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
,
H^{\sharp}f
acts as
Hf_{{\sf o}}
on objects.

Proof 3.4
.

First, notice that the construct
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
described above satisfies

HX_{{\sf o}}(A^{\prime},A)\leq H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)

(29)

for all objects
A^{\prime},A
.
In particular,
e\leq H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}(A,A)
holds.
Next, the inequality
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)\otimes H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime\prime},A^{\prime})\leq H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime\prime},A)
can be established by path concatenation, using that
HX_{{\sf o}}
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category. Hence
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category.

We will verify that
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
as above is indeed
{N}\mathop{*}{(HF\!_{\mathscr{X}})}
. Let
c_{\mathscr{X}}:HX_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
be the identity on objects. It is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor by (
29
).

We show now that (
24
) holds. Let
r
be an arbitrary element of the quantale and fix an object
C
in
HX_{r}
. Then by employing the path

\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{Hd^{r}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{Hd^{r}_{1}}
\textstyle{Hd^{r}_{0}(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
,
\textstyle{Hd^{r}_{0}(C)}
\textstyle{Hd^{r}_{1}(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
,
\textstyle{Hd^{r}_{1}(C)}

one can see that

\displaystyle r=

\displaystyle\ e\otimes r\otimes e

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ HX_{{\sf o}}(Hd^{r}_{0}(C),Hd^{r}_{0}(C))\otimes r\otimes HX_{{\sf o}}(Hd^{r}_{1}(C),Hd^{r}_{1}(C))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}(Hd^{r}_{0}(C),Hd^{r}_{1}(C))

We check the 1-dimensional aspect of the colimit: Let
f:HX_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
be a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor such that (
23
) holds.
Then the object-assignment
A\mapsto f(A)
(uniquely!) extends to a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
f^{\sharp}:H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
such that
f^{\sharp}c_{\mathscr{X}}=f
.
This is because for each path
(A^{\prime}=A^{\prime}_{0},A_{0}),(A^{\prime}_{1},A_{1}),\ldots,(A^{\prime}_{n},A_{n}=A)
and each tuple of elements
(r_{1},\ldots,r_{n})
we have that

\displaystyle HX_{{\sf o}}(A_{0}^{\prime},A_{0})\otimes r_{1}\otimes HX_{{\sf o}}(A^{\prime}_{1},A_{1})\otimes\ldots\otimes r_{n}\otimes HX_{{\sf o}}(A^{\prime}_{n},A_{n})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle HX_{{\sf o}}(A_{0}^{\prime},Hd^{r_{1}}_{0}(C_{1}))\otimes r_{1}\otimes HX_{{\sf o}}(Hd^{r_{1}}_{1}(C_{1}),Hd^{r_{2}}_{0}(C_{2}))\otimes\ldots\otimes r_{n}\otimes HX_{{\sf o}}(Hd^{r_{n}}_{0}(C_{n}),A_{n})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathscr{Y}}(f(A_{0}^{\prime}),fHd^{r_{1}}_{0}(C_{1}))\otimes{\mathscr{Y}}(fHd^{r_{1}}_{0}(C_{1}),fHd^{r_{1}}_{1}(C_{1}))\otimes{\mathscr{Y}}(fHd^{r_{1}}_{1}(C_{1}),fHd^{r_{2}}_{0}(C_{2}))\otimes\ldots

\displaystyle\otimes{\mathscr{Y}}(fHd^{r_{n}}_{0}(C_{n}),fHd^{r_{n}}_{1}(C_{n}))\otimes{\mathscr{Y}}(fHd^{r_{n}}_{0}(C_{n}),f(A_{n}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\mathscr{Y}}(f(A^{\prime}),f(A))

where we used that
f
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor and (
23
).
This proves that

H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)\leq{\mathscr{Y}}(f^{\sharp}(A^{\prime}),f^{\sharp}(A))

holds for all objects
A^{\prime},A
.

Finally, the 2-dimensional aspect of the colimit (
25
) is trivial, because
c_{\mathscr{X}}
is the identity on objects.

Hence we have proved that
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}={N}\mathop{*}{(HF\!_{\mathscr{X}})}
.
The action of

H^{\sharp}
on

{\mathscr{V}}
-functors is as follows:
For
f:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
,

H^{\sharp}f:H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{Y}}
is the unique
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor satisfying

(H^{\sharp}f)c_{\mathscr{X}}=c_{\mathscr{Y}}(Hf_{{\sf o}})

Given that both
c_{\mathscr{X}}
and
c_{\mathscr{Y}}
are identity on objects, we see that
H^{\sharp}f
and
Hf_{{\sf o}}
coincide on objects.

Corollary 14
.

Every
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
has a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification
T_{\mathscr{V}}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.

Proof 3.5
.

Apply Theorem
11
to the composite
H=DT:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.

3.3.
When the unit of the left-Kan extension is an isomorphism

Recall from Proposition
4
that the discrete functor
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is fully faithful (hence left Kan extensions along
D
are genuine extensions) if and only if the quantale is integral. In particular, for
e<\top
, there is no guarantee that the unit
H\longrightarrow(\mathsf{Lan}_{D}H)D
of the left-Kan extension of
H
along
D
is an isomorphism.

The reason why
D
is not fully-faithful for non-integral
{\mathscr{V}}
is that, for a discrete
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}=DX
, the relation
X_{\top}
as defined in Proposition
9
is empty in case
e<\top
, which in turn forces the self-distances in
(\mathsf{Lan}_{D}H)D\emptyset
to be
\top
whereas self-distances in
H\emptyset
may be
<\top
.

In this section we shall see necessary and sufficient conditions for
{\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}H
to coincide with
H
on discrete
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories.

Recall that
{\mathbb{1}}
is the one-element
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
with self-distance
e
and
{\mathbb{1}}_{\top}
is the one-element
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
with self-distance
\top
. We use the same notation to denote the respectice constant functors
{\mathbb{1}}:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
and
{\mathbb{1}}_{\top}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.
The main technical observation is contained in the following

{exa}

The left Kan extension of the functor
{\mathbb{1}}:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is the functor
{\mathbb{1}}_{\top}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.

{defi}

A
constant
of a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
K:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation
{\mathbb{1}}_{\top}\longrightarrow K
. A
constant
of a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation
{\mathbb{1}}\longrightarrow H
.

Remark 15
.

The constants of a functor
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
form a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category in which all self-distances are
\top
. —
Let
\tau:{\mathbb{1}}_{\top}\longrightarrow K
be a constant. Then each
\tau_{\mathscr{X}}:{\mathbb{1}}_{\top}\longrightarrow K{\mathscr{X}}
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor, picking an object
\tau_{\mathscr{X}}(0)
in
K{\mathscr{X}}
with

{\mathbb{1}}_{\top}(0,0)=\top\leq K{\mathscr{X}}(\tau_{\mathscr{X}}(0),\tau_{\mathscr{X}}(0))

hence we obtain the self-distance

[{\mathbb{1}}_{\top},K{\mathscr{X}}](\tau_{\mathscr{X}},\tau_{\mathscr{X}})=\top

and consequently

[{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}]({\mathbb{1}}_{\top},K)(\tau,\tau)=\bigwedge_{{\mathscr{X}}}[{\mathbb{1}}_{\top},K{\mathscr{X}}](\tau_{\mathscr{X}},\tau_{\mathscr{X}})=\top

Proposition 16
.

The
{\mathscr{V}}
-category of constants of a functor
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is isomorphic to its value on the empty
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.

Proof 3.6
.

There is an isomorphism of
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories

[{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}]({\mathbb{1}}_{\top},K)\cong[{\mathsf{Set}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}]({\mathbb{1}},KD)\cong[{\mathsf{Set}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}]({\mathsf{Set}}(\emptyset,-),KD)\cong KD\emptyset

where the the first isomorphism is due to Example
3.3
and the last isomorphism is the Yoneda lemma.

The remark and the proposition combine to the result that, for any
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
K:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
, the self-distances in
KD\emptyset
must be
\top
. In particular, we have

Corollary 17
.

If
e<\top
, then there is no constant functor
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
with value
{\mathbb{1}}
, or with value
DX
for any
X\in{\mathsf{Set}}
.

More generally, what is at stake here is that
e
is not a retract of
\top
, see the discussion towards the end of
[
40
, Ch.3.9]
.

Essentially the same argument as for Proposition
16
also proves

Proposition 18
.

The
{\mathscr{V}}
-category of constants of a functor
{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is isomorphic to its value on the empty set.

To summarise, for any functor
K:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
, the constants of
K
are isomorphic to the constants of
KD
, and isomorphic to
KD\emptyset
.
In particular, self-distances on elements of
KD\emptyset
are
\top
.

Therefore, in order to have
H\cong({\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}H)D
, self-distances in
H\emptyset
must be
\top
.
We are now going to show that this necessary condition is also sufficient.

An analysis of why, in Example
3.3
, the left Kan extension of
{\mathbb{1}}
along
D
is
{\mathbb{1}}_{\top}
but not
{\mathbb{1}}
, suggests that the reason for the failure of the unit being an isomorphism resides in the fact that
{\mathbb{1}}
is not a cocone over the
{\mathscr{V}}
-nerve
(N,{\mathbb{1}})
of
{\mathbb{1}}
. This leads to

{defi}

The functor
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}

preserves
{\mathscr{V}}
-nerves of sets
if the identity
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor on
HX
induces a cocone of
HX
over the diagram
(N,HF_{DX})
for each
X
.

Remark 19
.

(1)

H
preserves
{\mathscr{V}}
-nerves of sets iff
{\mathsf{Id}}_{HX}
satisfies (
23
), that is, iff

r\leq\bigwedge_{C\in H(DX)_{r}}HX(Hd^{r}_{0}(C),Hd^{r}_{1}(C))

for all
r\in{\mathscr{V}}
.

(2)

If
H
preserves
{\mathscr{V}}
-nerves of sets, then by the universal property of the colimit there is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
\beta_{X}:({\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}H)DX\longrightarrow HX
such that the identity of
HX
factorises as

\textstyle{HX\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{X}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{Id}}}
\textstyle{H^{\sharp}DX\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta_{X}}
\textstyle{HX}

Since
\alpha_{X}
is the identity on objects,
\beta_{X}
will again be so. Moreover, because both
\alpha_{X}
and
\beta_{X}
are
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors, the inequalities

HX(A^{\prime},A)\leq H^{\sharp}DX(A^{\prime},A)\leq HX(A^{\prime},A)

hold for each
A^{\prime},A
. Hence
({\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}H)DX(A^{\prime},A)=HX(A^{\prime},A)
, that is, the unit of the left Kan extension
\alpha_{X}
is an isomorphism (actually the identity).

Theorem 20
.

Let
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
a functor.
The following are equivalent.

(1)

The unit of the left-Kan extension
H\longrightarrow({\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}H)D
is an isomorphism.

(2)

Self-distances on elements of
H\emptyset
are
\top
.

(3)

All self-distances of all constants of
H
are
\top
.

(4)

H
preserves
{\mathscr{V}}
-nerves of sets.

These conditions always hold if
{\mathscr{V}}
is integral, or, for general
{\mathscr{V}}
, if
H\emptyset=\emptyset
.

Proof 3.7
.

For
(1)\Rightarrow(3)
and
(2)\Leftrightarrow(3)
see the comment after Proposition
18
.
(4)\Rightarrow(1)
has been established in Remark
19
. For
(2)\Rightarrow(4)
, let
X
be an arbitrary set and assume that
(2)
holds.
We will show that the identity of
HX
satisfies (
23
)

r\leq\bigwedge_{C\in H(DX)_{r}}HX(Hd^{r}_{0}(C),Hd^{r}_{1}(C))

for each
r
, hence
H
preserves
{\mathscr{V}}
-nerves of sets by Remark
19
.
We start by examining the the associated binary relations (
r
-level sets) of the (discrete)
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
DX
:

(DX)_{r}=\begin{cases}X\times X&r=\bot\\
\Delta_{X}=\{(x,x)\mid x\in X\}&\bot<r\leq e\\
\emptyset&\mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

Condition (
23
) is automatically satisfied for
r=\bot
.

If
\bot<r\leq e
, then
(DX)_{r}
is the diagonal of
X
and
d_{0}^{r}=d^{r}_{1}
is a bijection. Hence
Hd_{0}^{r}=Hd_{1}^{r}
, being an isomorphism of
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories, implies that

r\leq e\leq\bigwedge_{C\in H\Delta_{X}}H\Delta_{X}(C,C)=\bigwedge_{C\in H(DX)_{r}}HX(Hd^{r}_{0}(C),Hd^{r}_{1}(C))

proving that (
23
) holds for
\bot<r\leq e
.

We will now consider the case
r\nleq e
. Then
(DX)_{r}=\emptyset
and
d^{r}_{0}=d^{r}_{1}
is the unique map
!:\emptyset\longrightarrow X
. By hypothesis,
H\emptyset
has all self-
{\mathscr{V}}
-distances equal to
\top
. Then

r\leq\top=\bigwedge_{C\in H\emptyset}H\emptyset(C,C)\leq\bigwedge_{C\in H\emptyset}HX(H!(C),H!(C))=\bigwedge_{C\in H(DX)_{r}}HX(Hd^{r}_{0}(C),Hd^{r}_{1}(C))

which establishes (
23
) and, therefore, that
H
preserves
{\mathscr{V}}
-nerves of sets.

3.4.
Characterisation theorems and density

There is a well-known connection between left Kan extensions and colimits. For example, the finitary (that is, filtered colimit preserving) functors
{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
are precisely those which are left Kan extensions of their restrictions along the inclusion of finite sets

\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Set}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Fin}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

(30)

In this section the functors that arise as
{\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}H

\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H}
\scriptstyle{D}

(31)

are characterised as those functors that preserve
{\mathscr{V}}
-nerves (
20
). The proof proceeds by showing that the colimits exhibited in (
20
) and subsequently in Proposition
9
constitute a density presentation of
D

[
40
, Section 5.4]
.

The functors
{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
that arise as left Kan extensions in diagram (
30
) are precisely those that have a presentation by finitary operations and equations. Note that the elements
n\in{\mathsf{Fin}}
appear as the arities
X^{n}\longrightarrow X
of the operations of the presentation. Similarly, we may speak of the functors that arise as left Kan extensions in diagram (
31
) as functors that have a
presentation in discrete arities
, with the presentation given by the usual formula
({\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}H){\mathscr{X}}=\int^{S}{\mathscr{X}}^{DS}\otimes HS
,
16
16

16

Note that although
{\mathsf{Set}}
is not small, the coend does exist, by
[
40
, Section 4.2]
.
but which is more easily computed by applying
H
to the
{\mathscr{V}}
-nerve as in Equation (
22
).

A complication, studied in Proposition
4
, is that
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is not fully faithful if
{\mathscr{V}}
is not integral, that is, if
e<\top
.

Nevertheless we know from Corollary
12
, even without restricting to integral
{\mathscr{V}}
, that the discrete functor
{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is dense. Here we show that this result also follows for general reasons that do not depend on the particular presentation of
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories as colimits of
{\mathscr{V}}
-nerves.

Proposition 21
.

The
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is dense.

Proof 3.8
.

Let
\mathbb{I}
denote the unit
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-category (which has only one object
*
and
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-hom
\mathbb{I}(*,*)=\mathbb{1}
).
Consider the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functors
F:\mathbb{I}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}},\quad F(*)=\underline{1}
, where
\underline{1}
denotes a one-element set, and
G:\mathbb{I}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}},\quad G(*)=\mathbb{1}
. Then the usual coend formula gives
\mathsf{Lan}_{F}G=D
, using that
\mathbb{I}
is small and
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is cocomplete. Now by
[
40
, (5.17)]
,
G
is dense, and by
[
40
, Proposition 5.10]
,
D
is also dense, given that
F
is fully faithful.

The result on density presentations in
[
40
, Chapter 5]
requires Kan extensions along fully faithful functors. We will henceforth demand in the remaining of this Section that the quantale
{\mathscr{V}}
is integral, that is,
e=\top
, see Proposition
4
.

Recall from Proposition
9
that every
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
is the colimit
N\ast DF_{\mathscr{X}}
of the diagram
(N,DF_{\mathscr{X}})
of discrete
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories.

Theorem 22
.

The
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is dense and the diagrams
(N,DF_{\mathscr{X}})
form a density presentation of
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.

Proof 3.9
.

We know that
D
is dense from Proposition
21
. Using Proposition
9
, we see that all
{\mathscr{V}}
-coinserters
N\ast DF_{\mathscr{X}}
exist in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
and the category

{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is the closure of
{\mathsf{Set}}
under these colimits. By
[
40
, Theorem 5.19]
, in order to establish the density presentation of
D
, it remains to show that the colimits

N\ast DF_{\mathscr{X}}
are preserved by the functors

{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DS,-):{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
for all sets
S
.
Abbreviate as earlier
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DS,{\mathscr{X}})={\mathscr{X}}^{DS}
. Going back to the notation of the proof of Proposition
9
, we notice that
(DX_{r})^{DS}=({\mathscr{X}}^{DS})_{r}
and
(DX_{{\sf o}})^{DS}=({\mathscr{X}}^{DS})_{{\sf o}}
, so that the diagram
(DF_{\mathscr{X}})^{DS}

\textstyle{(DX_{r})^{DS}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(Dd_{1}^{r})^{DS}}
\scriptstyle{(Dd_{0}^{r})^{DS}}
\textstyle{(DX_{{\sf o}})^{DS}}

is equally the diagram
DF_{{\mathscr{X}}^{DS}}

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 19.307pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{({\mathscr{X}}^{DS})r\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.10966pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.307pt\raise 2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.10966pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.307pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 73.307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{({\mathscr{X}}^{DS})_{{\sf o}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

This gives us, using Proposition
9
, that

\displaystyle{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DS,N\ast DF_{\mathscr{X}})

\displaystyle\cong{\mathscr{X}}^{DS}

\displaystyle\cong N\ast DF_{{\mathscr{X}}^{DS}}

\displaystyle\cong N\ast(DF_{\mathscr{X}})^{DS}

\displaystyle\cong N\ast{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DS,DF_{\mathscr{X}})

showing that
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DS,-)
preserves
N\ast DF_{\mathscr{X}}
.

We next characterise the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functors with presentations in discrete arities, that is, the functors that are left Kan extensions of their restrictions along
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
, as those functors that preserve colimits of
{\mathscr{V}}
-nerves.

Theorem 23

(Characterisation of
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functors with presentations in discrete arities)
.

For
G:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
the following are equivalent:

(1)

There exists a functor
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
such that

G\cong{\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}H
.

(2)

G
preserves the colimits of
all diagrams
(N,DF_{\mathscr{X}})
.

Proof 3.10
.

Since the
(N,DF_{\mathscr{X}})
form a density presentation (Theorem
22
), this is the equivalence of items (i) and (iii) of
[
40
, Theorem 5.29]
.

{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ifications are similarly characterised but additionally preserve discreteness.

Theorem 24

(Characterisation of
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ifications)
.

For
G:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
the following are equivalent.

(1)

There exists a functor
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
such that

G\cong{\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}(DT)
, that is,
G
is
T_{\mathscr{V}}
, the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of
T
.

(2)

G
preserves the colimits of
all diagrams
(N,DF_{\mathscr{X}})
, and
G
preserves discrete
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories.

Proof 3.11
.

We use Theorem
23
and observe that
G
mapping discrete
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories to discrete
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories is equivalent to
GD
factoring as
DT
for some
T
.

{exa}

We will now give an example of a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
G:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
which is not a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification, even though it preserves discreteness of
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories.
G
is presented in non-discrete arities as follows: For a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
, put
G{\mathscr{X}}={\mathscr{X}}^{{\mathbb{2}}_{r}}
, where the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathbb{2}}_{r}
was introduced in (
11
), for
r
an element of the quantale.
Notice that
G
is the identity on discrete
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories, for every
\bot<r\leq e
.
Hence, if
G
was
{\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}(DT)
for some
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
, we could choose
T
to be the identity and then
G
would also have to be the identity, by Theorem
22
and
[
40
, Theorem 5.1]
.

3.5.
Extending polynomial and finitary functors

Let now
{\mathscr{V}}
be an arbitrary commutative quantale. We show that functors defined by finite powers and colimits extend from
{\mathsf{Set}}
to
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
via their universal properties. In particular, the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of a finitary functor is presented by the same operations and equations as its underlying
{\mathsf{Set}}
-functor.

{exas}

[
The
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of polynomial functors]

(1)

Let
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
, be a constant functor at a set
S
. Then
T_{\mathscr{V}}
acts as follows: For any
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
,
T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}
is the constant
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor to the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category with
S
as set of objects, with
{\mathscr{V}}
-distances

T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)=\begin{cases}\,\top\ \ ,\ \ x^{\prime}=x\\
\bot\ \ ,\ \ \mbox{otherwise}\end{cases}

That is,
T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}=S\cdot{\mathbb{1}}_{\top}
is the coproduct in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
of
S
copies of the terminal
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathbb{1}}_{\top}
.
Observe that in case
{\mathscr{V}}
is integral, we obtain
T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}=DS
for any
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
.

(2)

Let
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
be the functor
TX=X^{n}
, for
n
a natural number.
Then
T_{\mathscr{V}}
maps a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
to its
n
-th power
{\mathscr{X}}^{n}
, where an easy computation shows that

{\mathscr{X}}^{n}((x^{\prime}_{0},\dots,x^{\prime}_{n-1}),(x_{0},\dots,x_{n-1}))={\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime}_{0},x_{0})\wedge\dots\wedge{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime}_{n-1},x_{n-1}).

(32)

(3)

If
n
is an
arbitrary
cardinal number, the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification
T_{\mathscr{V}}
of

T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
,
TX=X^{n}
also exists and
T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}((x^{\prime}_{i}),(x_{i}))=\bigwedge_{i}{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime}_{i},x_{i})
. That is,
T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}={\mathscr{X}}^{n}
.

(4)

The
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of a finitary polynomial functor

X\mapsto\coprod_{n}X^{n}\bullet\Sigma n

is the “strongly polynomial”
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor

{\mathscr{X}}\mapsto\coprod_{n}{\mathscr{X}}^{n}\otimes D\Sigma n
, where
n
ranges through finite sets.
In particular, the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of the list functor
LX=X^{*}=\coprod_{n}X^{n}
maps a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
to the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category having as objects tuples of objects of
{\mathscr{X}}
, with non-trivial
{\mathscr{V}}
-distances only between tuples of same order, given by (
32
).

Our next aim is to show that quotients of polynomial functors can be
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ified by taking the “same” quotients in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.

Remark 25
.

The
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
preserves conical
colimits.
This follows from the
D_{o}
being an
ordinary
left adjoint.
However, the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is not
a left
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-adjoint
, as its ordinary right adjoint functor
V
cannot be extended to a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor.

Proposition 26
.

The assignment
({-})_{\mathscr{V}}:[{\mathsf{Set}},{\mathsf{Set}}]\longrightarrow[{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}]
,
T\mapsto T_{\mathscr{V}}
of the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification
preserves all colimits preserved by
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
. In particular,
T\mapsto T_{\mathscr{V}}
preserves conical colimits.

Proof 3.12
.

Any natural transformation
\tau:T\longrightarrow S
induces a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation

(\tau_{\mathscr{V}})_{\mathscr{X}}={N}\mathop{*}{(D\tau F\!_{\mathscr{X}})}:{N}\mathop{*}{(DTF\!_{\mathscr{X}})}\longrightarrow{N}\mathop{*}{(DSF\!_{\mathscr{X}})}

Since any colimit is cocontinuous in its weight and since

{N}\mathop{*}{(DTF\!_{\mathscr{X}})}\cong{(DTF\!_{\mathscr{X}})}\mathop{*}{N}

holds
[
40
, Section 3.1]
, the assignment
T\mapsto T_{\mathscr{V}}
preserves all colimits that are preserved by
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.
The last statement follows from Remark
25
.

Corollary 27
.

Suppose that the coequalizer

\textstyle{T_{\Gamma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{T_{\Sigma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{T}

is the equational presentation of an accessible functor
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
.
Then the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification
T_{\mathscr{V}}
can be obtained as the coequalizer

\textstyle{(T_{\Gamma})_{\mathscr{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathscr{V}}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{\mathscr{V}}}
\textstyle{(T_{\Sigma})_{\mathscr{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{\mathscr{V}}}
\textstyle{T_{\mathscr{V}}}

in
[{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}]
.

Remark 28

(
The
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of finitary functors).

Corollary
27
allows us to say that the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification
T_{\mathscr{V}}
of a finitary functor
T
presented by operations and equations is given by imposing the “same” operations and equations in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.
17
17

17

This explains
[
4
, Remark 6.5(1)]
.

This gives us an alternative way of computing the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of a finitary (or accessible) functor
T
:
First, one presents
T
as the quotient of a polynomial functor.
Then one extends the polynomial functor to
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
as in Example
3.5
.
Finally, one computes the relevant coequalizers to take a quotient in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
(see
[
69
, Proposition 2.11]
on coequalizers in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
).

{exa}

The finite powerset functor is a quotient of the list functor by the familiar equations stating that sets are invariant under the order and multiplicity of elements. It is shown in
[
65
]
that adding to these equations the requirement that lists are monotone, one obtains the finite convex powerset functor, which is indeed the posetification of the finite powerset functor
[
8
]
.

3.6.
Extending functors via relation lifting

Extending set-functors to various (ordered) categories using the so-called relation lifting is an established topic of research going back to
[
12
]
and
[
63
]
. Using the representation of quantale-enriched categories as relational presheaves of Section
2.2
, we will see here that this idea can also be applied to the extension of
{\mathsf{Set}}
-functors to
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
and, moreover, that
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ifications of weak-pullback preserving functors are more easily computed in this way.
In particular, (
34
) simplifies (
28
) significantly. As an application, we will compute the extension of the multiset functor and show that the well-known Pompeiu-Hausdorff metric arises as a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of the powerset functor.

As in
[
68
]
,
“what we do in effect is to reason about metric spaces (or other structures) in a universe where they
are
pre-orders, viz. in sheaves over
[0,\infty]
(or other
{\mathscr{V}}
).”
This point of view is summarised by Theorem
34
stating mild conditions under which the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification is obtained by applying the relation lifting to these preorders.

Remark 29

(Relation lifting, see for example
[
12
,
63
,
28
,
35
,
44
]
)
.

Let
\mathsf{Rel}
be the 2-category of sets with relations ordered by inclusion as arrows and
(-)_{\diamond}:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rel}
the functor mapping a function to its graph. Given a functor
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
the relation lifting
\mathsf{Rel}_{T}:\mathsf{Rel}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rel}
extends
T
to a colax functor

\textstyle{\mathsf{Rel}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{Rel}_{T}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Rel}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{(-)_{\diamond}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)_{\diamond}}

and is defined as follows. Let
p:Z\longrightarrow X
and
q:Z\longrightarrow Y
two arrows (called a span from
X
to
Y
). A
span
(p,q)
represents the relation

q_{\diamond}\cdot(p_{\diamond})^{-1}=\{(x,y)\mid\mbox{ there is $z\in Z$ s.t. $x=p(z)$ and $q(z)=y$}\}

(33)

where
\cdot
is composition and
-1
is relational inverse.
Composition of two relations is represented by the pullback of their spans.
Let
R
be a relation represented by the span
(p,q)
.
Then
\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(R)
is the relation represented by
(Tp,Tq)
.
This definition is independent of the choice of span.
In the following we only need to know that
\mathsf{Rel}_{T}
is monotone, preserves identities, and that it preserves function composition iff
T
preserves weak pullbacks. ∎

The following lemma considerably simplifies the formula in Corollary
13
for computing the left-Kan extension
{\mathrm{Lan}}_{D}H
in case
H=DT
with
T
preserving weak pullbacks.

Lemma 30
.

Let
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
be a weak pullback preserving functor. Then the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of
T
is given by

T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)=\bigvee_{s}\{s\mid(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{s})\}

(34)

Proof 3.13
.

To show “
\geq
” assume
(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{s})
.
Then there is some
C\in T(X_{s})
such that
Td_{0}^{s}(C)=A^{\prime}
and
Td_{1}^{s}(C)=A
. Since this is a (very special) zig-zag of the form of Corollary
13
it follows that
T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)\geq s
.

Conversely, for “
\leq
”, consider a zig-zag

(A^{\prime},A_{0},C_{1}\in DTX_{r_{1}},A^{\prime}_{1},A_{2},C_{1}\in DTX_{r_{2}},\ldots,A_{n}^{\prime})

as in Corollary
13
, contributing with

r=r_{1}\otimes\ldots r_{n}

to the distance
T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)
.
We want to see that

(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r}).

Note that
A^{\prime}=A_{0}
and
A^{\prime}_{i}=A_{i+1}
,
0<i<n
, and
A^{\prime}_{n}=A
, so that we are looking at the zig-zag

\textstyle{DTX_{r_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{DTd^{r_{1}}_{0}}
\textstyle{DTX_{r_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\quad\cdots\quad\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\cdots}
\textstyle{DTX_{r_{n}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{DTd^{r_{n}}_{1}}
\textstyle{DTX}
\textstyle{DTX}
\textstyle{DTX}
\textstyle{DTX}
\textstyle{DTX}

(35)

Consequently,
(A^{\prime},A)
is in the relation represented by the zig-zag (
35
), that is, in

\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r_{1}})\cdot\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r_{2}})\cdot\ldots\cdot\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r_{n}})

We calculate

\displaystyle\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r_{1}})\cdot\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r_{2}})\cdot\ldots\cdot\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r_{n}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r_{1}}\cdot X_{r_{2}}\cdot\ldots\cdot X_{r_{n}})

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r_{1}\otimes r_{2}\otimes\ldots\otimes r_{n}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r})

where the first step is due to
T
preserving weak pullbacks and the second step is due to
X_{\_}
being lax monoidal, see (
7
).

{exa}

[
The
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of the multiset functor]
The (finitary) multiset functor
M:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
can be presented as
MX=\coprod_{n}X^{n}/S_{n}
, where
S_{n}
denotes the full
n
-permutation group
[
33
]
.
Denote by
\widehat{[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]}
the equivalence class (multiset) of the tuple
(x_{1},...,x_{n})
under the action of
S_{n}
.

By Lemma
30
and Equation (
33
), the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification
M_{\mathscr{V}}
admits the following description: it maps a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
to the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category having as objects multisets of objects of
{\mathscr{X}}
– formally,
(M_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}})_{{\sf o}}=MX_{{\sf o}}
,
and the only possible non-trivial
{\mathscr{V}}
-distances are between multisets of same cardinal, namely

\displaystyle M_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(\widehat{[x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{n}]},\widehat{[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigvee_{s}\{s\mid\mbox{ there is }C\in MX_{s}\mbox{ s.t. }Md_{0}^{s}(C)=\widehat{[x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{n}]}

\displaystyle\mbox{ and }Md_{1}^{s}(C)=\widehat{[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]}\,\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigvee_{s}\{s\mid\mbox{ there is }\sigma\in S_{n}\mbox{ s.t. }s\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime}_{i},x_{\sigma(i)})

\displaystyle\mbox{ for all }i=1,n\,\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigvee_{\sigma\in S_{n}}\bigvee_{s}\{s\mid s\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime}_{i},x_{\sigma(i)})\mbox{ for all }i=1,n\,\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigvee_{\sigma\in S_{n}}\bigwedge_{i}{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime}_{i},x_{\sigma(i)})

The reader might notice that for
{\mathscr{V}}=([0,\infty],\geq_{\mathbb{R}}+,0)
, we regain by the above formula the matching metric on multisets
[
22
]
employed in image recognition techniques.
Also, it generalises
[
20
]
, where the case
{\mathscr{V}}={\mathbb{2}}
(that is,
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}={\mathsf{Preord}}
) was considered, in the sense that given a (pre)ordered set
(X,\leq)
, multisets on
X
are ordered by

\widehat{[x^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,x^{\prime}_{n}]}\leq\widehat{[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]}\Longleftrightarrow\mbox{ there is }\sigma\in S_{n}\mbox{ s.t. }x^{\prime}_{i}\leq x_{\sigma(i)}\mbox{ for all }i=1,n

In fact,
[
20
]
corroborated with Lemma
30
and (
33
) gives us the recipe to compute the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of any analytic
{\mathsf{Set}}
-functor,
18
18

18

Analytic functors
{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
preserve weak pullbacks
[
33
, Lemma 1.8]
.

not just of the multiset functor.

{exa}

[The
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of the powerset]
Let
\mathcal{P}:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
be the powerset functor.
Using Lemma
30
and Equation (
33
), the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification
\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{V}}
is described as follows.
Let
{\mathscr{X}}
be any small
{\mathscr{V}}
-category.
Then the objects of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}
are subsets of the set of objects of
{\mathscr{X}}
, while the
{\mathscr{V}}
-“distances” in
\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}
are computed as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigvee_{s}\{s\mid\mbox{ there is $C$ in $\mathcal{P}X_{s}$ s.t. $\mathcal{P}d_{0}^{s}(C)=A^{\prime}$ and $\mathcal{P}d_{1}^{s}(C)=A$}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigvee_{s}\{s\mid\forall\,x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}\,\exists\,x\in A.\,s\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\mbox{ and }\forall\,x\in A\,\exists\,x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}.\,s\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\}

Remembering that the join in
{\mathscr{V}}
denotes the infimum over all distances, we recognise the familiar Pompeiu-Hausdorff metric as seen in
[
11
, Definition 2.2]
, now generalised to categories enriched over an arbitrary commutative quantales
{\mathscr{V}}
. We will discuss the relationship to the more customary formulation in the following.
∎

Recall from
[
25
,
51
]
that a quantale (more generally, a poset)
{\mathscr{V}}
is called
completely distributive
if the function from the poset of downsets of
{\mathscr{V}}
to
{\mathscr{V}}
,
\mathfrak{D}\mapsto\bigvee\mathfrak{D}
, has a left adjoint. This left adjoint maps each
r\in{\mathscr{V}}
to the downset
\{s\mid s\ll r\}
of all elements totally-below it, where the “totally-below” relation is

s\ll r\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad(\,\forall\,\mathfrak{D}\subseteq{\mathscr{V}}\mbox{ downset such that}\,\,r\leq\bigvee\mathfrak{D}\Rightarrow\,s\in\mathfrak{D}\,)

Examples of completely distributive quantales are
{\mathscr{V}}=[0,1]
and
{\mathscr{V}}=[0,\infty]
.
For such a quantale, we provide below another characterisation of the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of the powerset functor:

Proposition 31

(The
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of the powerset, continued)
.

If the quantale
{\mathscr{V}}
is completely distributive, then the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of the powerset functor can be described as follows:
for a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
,
\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}
is the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category with objects
\mathcal{P}X_{{\sf o}}
, and
{\mathscr{V}}
-homs

\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)=\big(\bigwedge_{x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}}\bigvee_{x\in A}{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\big)\bigwedge\big(\bigwedge_{x\in A}\bigvee_{x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}}{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\big)

(36)

We will first need a lemma:

Lemma 32
.

Let
r\in{\mathscr{V}}
and
S\subseteq{\mathscr{V}}
. Then
r\ll\bigvee S
if and only if there is some
s\in S
with
r\ll s
.

Proof 3.14

(Proof of Lemma
32
)
.

The “if” implication is immediate. To see the other implication, assume that for all
s\in S
,
r\not\ll s
. That is, for each
s\in S
there is a downset
\mathfrak{D}_{s}
with
s\leq\bigvee\mathfrak{D}_{s}
and
r\not\in\mathfrak{D}_{s}
. But then
\bigcup_{s\in S}\mathfrak{D}_{s}
is again a downset and

\bigvee S\leq\bigvee\bigcup_{s\in S}\mathfrak{D}_{s}\quad\mbox{ but }r\not\in\bigcup_{s\in S}\mathfrak{D}_{s}

which contradicts
r\ll\bigvee S
.

We are now ready to prove Proposition
31
.

Proof 3.15

(Proof of Proposition
31
)
.

Remember that in Example
3.6
, we have obtained that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigvee_{s}\{s\mid\mbox{$\forall\,x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}\ \exists\,x\in A.\,s\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)$
and
$\forall\,x\in A\ \exists\,x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}.\,s\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)$}\}

Denote for simplicity

S=\{s\mid\mbox{
$\forall\,x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}\ \exists\,x\in A.\,s\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)$
and
$\forall\,x\in A\ \exists\,x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}.\,s\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)$
}\}

and notice that
S
is a downset; also put

r=\big(\bigwedge_{x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}}\bigvee_{x\in A}{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\big)\bigwedge\big(\bigwedge_{x\in A}\bigvee_{x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}}{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\big)

Thus we want to see that
r=\displaystyle{\bigvee}S
. The inequality “
\geq
” follows easily with no assumption on the quantale
{\mathscr{V}}
. For the other inequality, use complete distributivity to see that

r\leq\bigvee S

is equivalent with

\{s\mid s\ll r\}\subseteq S

Let now
s\ll r
.
Then for each
x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}
, it follows that
s\ll\bigvee_{x\in A}{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)
, thus by the above lemma we find some
x\in A
with
s\ll{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)
, which implies
s\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)
. Similarly, for all
x\in A
there is
x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}
with
s\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)
.
Consequently,
s\in S
and the proof is finished.

Remark 33
.

(1)

Let us switch notation to the dual order (that is, the natural “less-or-equal” order in case of the reals).
So we write
\inf
for
\bigvee
and
\sup
for
\bigwedge
, in order to emphasise the interpretation of
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
’s as metric spaces.
Then formula (
36
) becomes

\sup\{\ \sup_{x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}}\ \underset{x\in A}{\vphantom{p}\inf\vphantom{p}}\ {\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\ ,\ \sup_{x\in A}\ \underset{x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}}{\vphantom{p}\inf\vphantom{p}}\ {\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\}

(37)

As mentioned earlier, this metric given by Equation (
37
) above, is known as the Pompeiu-Hausdorff metric (
[
34
, § 28]
,
[
50
, § 21]
,
[
46
, § 21.VII]
).
We should mention also the connection with the work of
[
3
]
.
Finally, observe that in case
{\mathscr{V}}={\mathbb{2}}
(so
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}={\mathsf{Preord}}
), the above specialises to the locally monotone functor
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{2}}:{\mathsf{Preord}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Preord}}
which sends a preorder
(X,\leq)
to the Egli-Milner preorder

A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A\quad\mbox{iff}\quad\forall\,x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}\ \exists\,x\in A.\,x^{\prime}\leq x\mbox{ and }\forall\,x^{\prime}\in A\ \exists\,x\in A^{\prime}.\,x^{\prime}\leq x

on the powerset
\mathcal{P}X
.

(2)

Notice that the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of the powerset functor obtained in Proposition
31
for completely distributive quantale is the (symmetrised version of the) free cocompletion monad with respect to (the saturated class of) conical colimits in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}

[
61
]
.

Example
3.6
raises the question whether the relations
(T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}})_{r}
can be computed simply by applying the relation lifting
\mathsf{Rel}_{T}
to the
X_{r}
.
The next example shows that we need to be careful with this.

{exa}

Let
{\mathscr{X}}
be the metric space of real numbers considered as a generalised metric space over
{\mathscr{V}}=[0,\infty]
. Let
A^{\prime}
be the subset of irrational reals and
A
the subset of rational reals.
Then
\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)=0
but
(A^{\prime},A)\notin\mathsf{Rel}_{\mathcal{P}}(X_{0})
.
Indeed,
\mathsf{Rel}_{\mathcal{P}}(X_{0})
is the diagonal, since
X_{0}
is the diagonal.

In terms of Section
2.2
, the example above shows that the relational presheaf

\displaystyle(\Sigma{\mathscr{V}})^{\mathsf{coop}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ \mathsf{Rel}

\displaystyle r\ \ \

\displaystyle\ \mapsto\ \mathsf{Rel}_{\mathcal{P}}(X_{r})

is not continuous as we have
(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{\mathcal{P}}(X_{\varepsilon}),\varepsilon>_{\mathbb{R}}0
, but
(A^{\prime},A)\notin\mathsf{Rel}_{\mathcal{P}}(X_{0})
.
We can repair this defect by closing the presheaf as follows.
Given a relational presheaf
F:(\Sigma{\mathscr{V}})^{\mathsf{coop}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rel}
, and assuming that
{\mathscr{V}}
is completely distributive, define its closure as

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}F:(\Sigma{\mathscr{V}})^{\mathsf{coop}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ \mathsf{Rel}

\displaystyle*\ \ \

\displaystyle\ \mapsto F(*)

\displaystyle r\ \ \

\displaystyle\ \mapsto\ \bigcap\{F(s)\mid s\ll r\}

It is immediate from the respective definitions that
\mathcal{C}F
is a continuous relational presheaf, assuming the tensor laxly preserves the totally-below relation, in the sense that
r\ll s
and
r^{\prime}\ll s^{\prime}
imply
s\otimes s^{\prime}\ll r\otimes r^{\prime}
.
Also, that
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{C}F)=\mathcal{C}F
.
That is,
\mathcal{C}
acts like a closure operator on relational presheaves.
This construct extends to a functor
\mathcal{C}:\mathsf{RelPresh}\longrightarrow\mathsf{RelPresh}_{c}
.

The idea that the
