{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of a weak pullback preserving
{\mathsf{Set}}
-functor
T
can be computed on a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
by applying the relation lifting of
T
to the “uniformity”
(X_{r})_{r\in{\mathscr{V}}}
of
{\mathscr{X}}
can now be formalised as in next result.
It is a consequence of Proposition
2.2
and of Lemma
30
.

Theorem 34
.

Suppose
{\mathscr{V}}
is a completely distributive quantale, such that the tensor laxly preserves the totally-below relation.
Let
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
be a functor which preserves weak pullbacks.
Then post-composition with
\mathsf{Rel}_{T}:\mathsf{Rel}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Rel}
yields a functor

\mathsf{Rel}_{T}\circ(-):\mathsf{RelPresh}_{c}\longrightarrow\mathsf{RelPresh}

such that the diagram

\textstyle{\mathsf{RelPresh}_{c}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{Rel}_{T}\circ(-)}
\textstyle{\mathsf{RelPresh}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{C}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{RelPresh}_{c}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{D}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D}
\scriptstyle{{\mathsf{Id}}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D}

commutes.
Moreover, the composite in the upper row is the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of
T
.

Proof 3.16
.

Let
{\mathscr{X}}
be a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category and let
\Phi({\mathscr{X}})
be its continuous relational presheaf. We compute

\displaystyle(A^{\prime},A)\in(T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}})_{r}

\displaystyle\ \Longleftrightarrow\ r\leq\bigvee_{s}\{s\mid(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{s})\}

\displaystyle\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \forall s\,.\,s\ll r\,\Longrightarrow\,(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{s})

\displaystyle\ \Longleftrightarrow\ (A^{\prime},A)\in\bigcap\{\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{s})\mid s\ll r\}

\displaystyle\ \Longleftrightarrow\ (A^{\prime},A)\in\mathcal{C}(\mathsf{Rel}_{T}\circ\Phi({\mathscr{X}}))(r)

where the respective steps are due to the following. (i) Lemma
30
. (ii) For all downsets
\mathfrak{D}
in a completely distributive
{\mathscr{V}}
one has
r\leq\bigvee\mathfrak{D}\Longleftrightarrow\forall s(s\ll r\Longrightarrow s\in\mathfrak{D})
. (iii) Definition of
\bigcap
. (iv) Definition of
\mathcal{C}
.

3.7.
Extending
{\mathsf{Set}}
-functors equipped with a
{\mathscr{V}}
-metric

In Section
4.2
, we will see that the behaviour of coalgebras for a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification is metrically trivial.
But often
{\mathsf{Set}}
-functors carry a natural metric and thus can be considered to be functors
{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
, which in turn produce
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-endofunctors with non-trivial metric coalgebraic behaviour.

{exa}

(1)

Consider
{\mathscr{V}}={\mathbb{2}}
and let
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Preord}}
be the powerset functor with the usual inclusion relation on subsets
HX=(\mathcal{P}(X),\subseteq)
. Then by
[
7
]
, the extension
H^{\sharp}
to
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}={\mathsf{Preord}}
maps a (pre)ordered set
(X,\leq)
to
\mathcal{P}(X)
, ordered with the lower half of the Egli-Milner order on subsets, namely

A^{\prime}\leq A\iff\forall\,x^{\prime}\in A^{\prime}\,.\,\exists\,x\in A\,.\,x^{\prime}\leq x

for all
A^{\prime},A\in\mathcal{P}(X)
.

(2)

Consider the functor
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
,
TX=X^{A}\times B
.
Recall that
T
-coalgebras are known as deterministic automata with input set
A
and outputs in
B
:
the transition map of a coalgebra
X\longrightarrow X^{A}\times B
sends a state
x\in X
to a pair:
the associated next-state function
next(x):A\longrightarrow X
that works when receiving an input from
A
, and an output
out(x)
in
B
.

Assume that the output set
B
carries the additional structure of a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category – that, is, there is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{B}}
with underlying set of objects
B
.
This is the case for example whenever there is a natural order on
B
, or a metric, which measures how far apart two outputs might be.

Then the ordinary functor
T_{o}
can be written as the composite
VH_{o}
, where
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
HX=DX^{A}\otimes{\mathscr{B}}
.
Now it is immediate to see that the latter extends to the functor
H^{\sharp}
on
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
over the “generalised metric space”
{\mathscr{B}}
, mapping a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
to the tensor product of
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}={\mathscr{X}}^{A}\otimes{\mathscr{B}}
, where
{\mathscr{X}}^{A}={\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DA,{\mathscr{X}})
.

There are two particular cases of this functor that are worth mentioning from a coalgebraic point of view:

(a)

We start with the easiest case, when
A
is a singleton.
The coalgebras for the functor
TX=X\times B
are usually called stream automata;
under the assumption that
B
carries the structure of a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{B}}
, we obtain as above
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}={\mathscr{X}}\otimes{\mathscr{B}}
the stream functor on
{\mathscr{V}}
-metric spaces.

(b)

If we take an arbitrary set of inputs
A
, but particularise
B
to
\{0,1\}
, then the transition function of a coalgebra
provides binary outputs, deciding if a state is accepting (response
1
) or not – that is,
T
-coalgebras are deterministic automata.
Enhance now the set of outputs with a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category structure
{\mathbb{2}}_{r,s}
generalising (
11
)

{\mathbb{2}}_{r,s}(0,0)={\mathbb{2}}_{r,s}(1,1)=e\,,\,{\mathbb{2}}_{r,s}(0,1)=r\,,\,{\mathbb{2}}_{r,s}(1,0)=s

where the elements
r,s
of the quantale satisfy
r\otimes s\leq e
, in order to produce the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-endofunctor
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}={\mathscr{X}}^{A}\otimes{\mathbb{2}}_{r,s}
.

The above examples are typical.
It happens quite often for endofunctors
T
on
{\mathsf{Set}}
to carry an interesting
{\mathscr{V}}
-metric. Then
TX
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category rather than a mere set.
The following generalises the notion of an order on a functor
[
38
]
from
{\mathbb{2}}
to an arbitrary quantale
{\mathscr{V}}
.

{defi}

Let
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
be a functor. We say that
T

carries a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt
-metric
if there is a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
such that
T_{o}
coincides with the composite

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 12.02917pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.02917pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}_{o}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.47986pt\raise 5.89168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.89168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{H_{o}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 36.02917pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 36.02917pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}_{o}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 75.34863pt\raise 5.39168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{V}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 93.57086pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 93.57086pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}_{o}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Let
T
and
H
be as in the above definition.
How are
T
and
H^{\sharp}
, the left Kan extension of
H
along
D
as provided by Theorem
11
, related?

Recall from Theorem
11
and Corollary
13
that the unit
\alpha:H\longrightarrow H^{\sharp}D
of the left Kan extension is the identity on objects, in particular that
\alpha
is a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural isomorphism in case
D
is fully faithful, that is, if the quantale is integral.
Hence
T_{o}=VH_{o}\cong VH_{o}^{\sharp}D_{o}
always holds; using now that the counit of the ordinary adjunction
D_{o}\dashv V
is again the identity on objects, we obtain an ordinary natural transformation

\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}_{o}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H^{\sharp}_{o}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}_{o}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}_{o}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{o}}
\scriptstyle{V}
\scriptstyle{\nearrow\beta}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}_{o}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V}

Proposition 35
.

The natural transformation
\beta
is component-wise bijective.

Consequently,
H^{\sharp}
is a lifting of
T
to
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
as in Definition
3.1
(
2
).

We will exhibit below another possible way of lifting
T
to
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.

{exa}

[
The Kantorovich lifting]

Let
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
be a functor and let
\heartsuit:T{\mathscr{V}}_{o}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
be an arbitrary map (a
{\mathscr{V}}
-valued predicate lifting), where by slight abuse we identify the quantale with its underlying set of elements.

Using the
{\mathscr{V}}
-valued predicate lifting
\heartsuit
, we can endow
T
with a
{\mathscr{V}}
-metric
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
as follows:
for each set
X
, put
HX
to be the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category with set of objects
TX
, and
{\mathscr{V}}
-distances

(HX)(A^{\prime},A)=\bigwedge_{h:X\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}_{o}}[(\heartsuit\circ Th)(A^{\prime}),(\heartsuit\circ Th)(A)]

(38)

Remark that the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
HX
is precisely the initial lift of
TX
with respect to the family of maps
\heartsuit\circ Th:TX\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
indexed by all
h:X\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
. In particular, the
{\mathscr{V}}
-distances
(HX)(A^{\prime},A)
are the greatest such that all maps
\heartsuit\circ Th
are actually
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors
HX\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}
.

For a map
f:X\longrightarrow Y
, we let
Hf
act as
Tf
on objects. It is easy to see that
Hf:HX\longrightarrow HY
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor.

The above defines a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}

(the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-enrichment being a consequence of
{\mathsf{Set}}
being free as a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-category) with
VH_{o}=T_{o}
, for every map
\heartsuit:T{\mathscr{V}}_{o}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
.

That is,
H
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-metric for
T
and we may consider the lifting of
T
given by the left Kan extension
H^{\sharp}
, as discussed above.
In order to obtain (possibly) another lifting of
T
to
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
, we make an additional assumption:
\heartsuit
is
{\mathscr{V}}
-monotone,
19
19

19

This generalises the notion of monotone predicate lifting from the two-element quantale to arbitrary
{\mathscr{V}}
, see
[
8
, Section 7]
.
in the following sense: for every set
X
and maps
h,k:X\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
, the inequality

\bigwedge_{x\in X}[h(x),k(x)]\leq\bigwedge_{A\in TX}[(\heartsuit\circ Th)(A),(\heartsuit\circ Tk)(A)]

(39)

holds.
20
20

20

In categorical terms, it says that the correspondence
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DX,{\mathscr{V}}){\rightarrow}{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(DTX,{\mathscr{V}})
,
h\mapsto\heartsuit\circ Th
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor.
In particular, taking
X=\emptyset
and
h=k
the unique map from the empty set, we see that
{\mathscr{V}}
-monotonicity of
\heartsuit
entails that self-distances on objects of
H\emptyset
are
\top
, hence
H^{\sharp}D=H
by Theorem
20
.

Consider the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
\bar{H}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
which maps a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
to the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
\bar{H}{\mathscr{X}}
with set of objects
TX_{{\sf o}}
, and
{\mathscr{V}}
-homs

\bar{H}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)=\bigwedge_{h:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}}[(\heartsuit\circ Th_{{\sf o}})(A^{\prime}),(\heartsuit\circ Th_{{\sf o}})(A)]

(40)

for every
A^{\prime},A
in
TX_{{\sf o}}
, where this time
h
ranges over
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors and the notation
h_{{\sf o}}
refers to the object assignment of a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
h
, as in item (
1
) of the proof of Theorem
11
.

As above for
H
, the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
\bar{H}{\mathscr{X}}
is precisely the initial lift of
TX_{{\sf o}}
with respect to the family of maps
\heartsuit\circ Th_{{\sf o}}:TX_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
(indexed by all
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors
h:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}
), in particular (
40
) provides the greatest
{\mathscr{V}}
-distances such that for all
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors
h:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}
, the composite maps
\heartsuit\circ Th_{{\sf o}}
are also
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors.

For
f:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor, let
\bar{H}f
act as
Tf_{{\sf o}}
on objects. Observe that it is the
{\mathscr{V}}
-monotonicity of
\heartsuit
which ensures that
\bar{H}
is indeed a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor.
It is clear that this
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
\bar{H}
is a lifting for
T
. The particular case
{\mathscr{V}}=[0,\infty]
provides a generalised Kantorovich lifting as in
[
10
]
.

Relations (
38
) and (
40
) ensure that for each
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
, the identity on objects is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
\bar{c}_{\mathscr{X}}:HX_{{\sf o}}\longrightarrow\bar{H}{\mathscr{X}}
. We will now prove that
\bar{c}_{\mathscr{X}}
satisfies (
23
), thus it induces a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
\gamma_{\mathscr{X}}:H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow\bar{H}{\mathscr{X}}
such that
\gamma_{\mathscr{X}}\circ c_{\mathscr{X}}=\bar{c}_{\mathscr{X}}
.

Let thus
r
an arbitrary element of the quantale. Then we have

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{C\in HX_{r}}\bar{H}{\mathscr{X}}(\bar{c}_{\mathscr{X}}Hd^{r}_{0}(C),\bar{c}_{\mathscr{X}}Hd^{r}_{1}(C))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r})}\bar{H}{\mathscr{X}}(\bar{c}_{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime}),\bar{c}_{\mathscr{X}}(A))

(41)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r})}\bigwedge_{h:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}}[\heartsuit\circ Th_{{\sf o}}(A^{\prime}),\heartsuit\circ Th_{{\sf o}}(A)]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{w\in T{\mathscr{V}}_{r}}[\heartsuit\circ Td^{r}_{0}(w),\heartsuit\circ Td^{r}_{1}(w)]

because

(Th_{{\sf o}}\times Th_{{\sf o}})(\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r}))\subseteq\mathsf{Rel}_{T}((h_{{\sf o}}\times h_{{\sf o}})(X_{r}))\subseteq\mathsf{Rel}_{T}({\mathscr{V}}_{r})

In the above, we have used that
h:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor and that relation lifting preserves inclusions, and denoted
{\mathscr{V}}_{r}=\{(s^{\prime},s)\mid r\leq[s^{\prime},s]\}
and
d^{r}_{0},d^{r}_{1}:{\mathscr{V}}_{r}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}_{{\sf o}}
as for any
{\mathscr{V}}
-category.

Using now (
39
) for the set
{\mathscr{V}}_{r}
and for the maps
d^{r}_{0},d^{r}_{1}
, we see that

r\leq\bigwedge_{(s^{\prime},s)\in{\mathscr{V}}_{r}}[d^{r}_{0}(s^{\prime},s),d^{r}_{1}(s^{\prime},s)]\leq\bigwedge_{w\in T{\mathscr{V}}_{r}}[\heartsuit\circ Td^{r}_{0}(w),\heartsuit\circ Td^{r}_{1}(w)]

(42)

Putting together (
41
) and (
42
) shows that
\bar{c}_{\mathscr{X}}
satisfies (
23
).

In conclusion, there is a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation
H^{\sharp}\longrightarrow\bar{H}
whose components act as identity on objects, that is, the
{\mathscr{V}}
-distances in
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}
are always smaller than in
\bar{H}{\mathscr{X}}
.

Corroborating the above with the particular example of the Hausdorff metric
\bar{H}
on the (finite) powerset functor
T=\mathcal{P}
exhibited in
[
10
]
, it raises the question under which conditions
\bar{H}\cong H^{\sharp}
holds, very much in the spirit of the Kantorovich duality
[
67
]
. Currently this aspect is still under research and will not be treated in this paper.
∎

3.8.
Closure properties of
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ifications

We have seen in Proposition
26
that
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ifications are closed under conical colimits.
In this section, we give sufficient conditions for the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ifications to be closed under composition and finite products.

We will first discuss when
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ifications are closed under composition:
Generalising a result from
[
8
, Remark 4.8(4)]
for the case
{\mathscr{V}}={\mathbb{2}}
, given weak pullback preserving functors
T,T^{\prime}:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
, we will see that the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of
TT^{\prime}
is the composite of the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ifications
T_{\mathscr{V}}
and
T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}
if the quantale
{\mathscr{V}}
satisfies the additional assumption of being completely distributive.

Observe that by the universal property of left Kan extensions, there is a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation
(TT^{\prime})_{\mathscr{V}}\longrightarrow T_{\mathscr{V}}T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}
, for any functors
T,T^{\prime}:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
.
The component of this natural transformation at a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
, namely the
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
(TT^{\prime})_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow T_{\mathscr{V}}T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}
, is easily seen to act as identity on objects,
21
21

21

Notice that both
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories
(TT^{\prime})_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}
and
T_{\mathscr{V}}T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}
share the same set of objects, namely
TT^{\prime}X_{{\sf o}}
.
and on
{\mathscr{V}}
-homs it provides the inequality

(TT^{\prime})_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)\leq T_{\mathscr{V}}T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)

for all objects
A^{\prime},A\in TT^{\prime}X_{{\sf o}}
.

Proposition 36
.

Assume that the quantale
{\mathscr{V}}
is completely distributive.
Let
T,T^{\prime}:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
be functors which preserve weak pullbacks.
Then the inequality

T_{\mathscr{V}}T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)\leq(TT^{\prime})_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)

holds for all
A^{\prime},A\in TT^{\prime}X_{{\sf o}}
.

Proof 3.17
.

Because
T
preserves weak pullbacks,
T_{\mathscr{V}}T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)=\bigvee\{r\mid(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}((T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}})_{r})\}
by Lemma
30
.
Let
r\in{\mathscr{V}}_{o}
such that
(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}((T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}})_{r})
. Given that
{\mathscr{V}}
is completely distributive, to show that

r\leq(TT^{\prime})_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(A^{\prime},A)=\bigvee\{s\mid(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{TT^{\prime}}(X_{s})\}

is the same as

\forall\,s\in{\mathscr{V}}_{o}\,.\,s\ll r\,\Longrightarrow(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{TT^{\prime}}(X_{s})

because
\{s\mid(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{TT^{\prime}}(X_{s})\}
is a downset and
{\mathscr{V}}
is completely distributive.
Also notice that

(T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}})_{r}\subseteq\mathsf{Rel}_{T}^{\prime}(X_{s})\ \ \

(43)

holds for all
s\ll r
. Indeed, for
(B^{\prime},B)\in(T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}})_{r}
, we have that

r\leq T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}(B^{\prime},B)=\bigvee\{s\mid(B^{\prime},B)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}^{\prime}(X_{s})\}

using that
T^{\prime}
preserves weak pullbacks and Lemma
30
.
But the relation above is equivalent to

\forall\,s\in{\mathscr{V}}_{o}\,.\,s\ll r\,\Longrightarrow(B^{\prime},B)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}^{\prime}(X_{s})

Hence
(T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}})_{r}\subseteq\mathsf{Rel}_{T}^{\prime}(X_{s})
holds for all
s\ll r
. Therefore we have

(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}((T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}})_{r})\subseteq\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(\mathsf{Rel}_{T^{\prime}}(X_{s}))=\mathsf{Rel}_{TT^{\prime}}(X_{s})

for all
s\ll r
, where the last equality follows from
[
16
, Section 4.4]
.

Corollary 37
.

Let
{\mathscr{V}}
be a completely distributive commutative quantale and
T,T^{\prime}:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
functors which preserve weak pullbacks. Then

(TT^{\prime})_{\mathscr{V}}=T_{\mathscr{V}}T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}

holds.

Next we show that the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification process is also closed under finite products, if some additional conditions are imposed to
{\mathscr{V}}
.

Proposition 38
.

Let
{\mathscr{V}}
be an integral commutative quantale such that finite meets distribute over arbitrary joins in
{\mathscr{V}}
(the underlying lattice of
{\mathscr{V}}
is a frame).
Then the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification assignment
({-})_{\mathscr{V}}:[{\mathsf{Set}},{\mathsf{Set}}]\longrightarrow[{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}]
,
T\mapsto T_{\mathscr{V}}
, preserves finite products of weak pullback preserving functors.

Proof 3.18
.

The empty product, that is, the constant functor
{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
at the one-element set has as
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification the constant functor at
{\mathbb{1}}
.
Because
e=\top
holds in
{\mathscr{V}}
by hypothesis, the latter is the terminal object in
[{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}]

[
15
]
.

Let us look now at binary products of weak-pullback preserving functors
T,T^{\prime}:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
. We will denote by
T\times{T^{\prime}}
the product in
[{\mathsf{Set}},{\mathsf{Set}}]
, sending
X
to
TX\times{T^{\prime}}X
and shall extend this notation to
[{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}]
.

Now observe that for a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
, the
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories
T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}\times T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}
and
(T\times{T^{\prime}})_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}
share the same objects.
The universal property of products induces a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor
(T\times T^{\prime})_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}\times T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}
, which on the corresponding
{\mathscr{V}}
-homs says that the inequality

(T\times{T^{\prime}})_{\mathscr{V}}({\mathscr{X}})((A^{\prime},B^{\prime}),(A,B))\leq(T_{\mathscr{V}}\times T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}})({\mathscr{X}})((A^{\prime},B^{\prime}),(A,B))

holds for each objects
A^{\prime},A
of
T_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}
and
B^{\prime},B
of
T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}{\mathscr{X}}
.
The converse inequality is shown below, using Lemma
30
and the frame law:

\displaystyle(T_{\mathscr{V}}\times T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}})({\mathscr{X}})((A^{\prime},B^{\prime}),(A,B))=

\displaystyle T_{\mathscr{V}}({\mathscr{X}})(A^{\prime},A)\wedge T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}({\mathscr{X}})(B^{\prime},B)=

\displaystyle\bigvee\{t\mid(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{t}))\}\wedge\bigvee\{s\mid(B^{\prime},B)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T^{\prime}}(X_{s}))\}=

\displaystyle\bigvee\{t\wedge s\mid(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{t}),(B^{\prime},B)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T^{\prime}}(X_{s})\}\leq

\displaystyle\bigvee\{t\wedge s\mid(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{t\wedge s}),(B^{\prime},B)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T^{\prime}}(X_{t\wedge s})\}\leq

\displaystyle\bigvee\{r\mid(A^{\prime},A)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T}(X_{r}),(B^{\prime},B)\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T^{\prime}}(X_{r})\}=

\displaystyle\bigvee\{r\mid((A^{\prime},B^{\prime}),(A,B))\in\mathsf{Rel}_{T\times T^{\prime}}(X_{r})\}=

\displaystyle(T\times T^{\prime})_{\mathscr{V}}({\mathscr{X}})((A^{\prime},B^{\prime}),(A,B))

That is,
(T\times T^{\prime})_{\mathscr{V}}=T_{\mathscr{V}}\times T^{\prime}_{\mathscr{V}}
holds for all functors
T,T^{\prime}
which preserve weak pullbacks.

4.
Solving domain equations across different base categories

In the previous section, we studied those functors on
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
that arise as left Kan extensions of functors on
{\mathsf{Set}}
, with Theorems
24
and
23
accounting for the situations depicted on the left and right below.

\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{T}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{D}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D}

\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{T}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D}
\scriptstyle{H}

In this section, we study the relationship between solving recursive equations in
{\mathsf{Set}}
and in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.
In both cases, accounted for by Theorems
52
and
53
, if we have a coalgebra
X\longrightarrow TX
or
X\longrightarrow VHX
, then two states in
X
are behaviourally equivalent (bisimilar) iff they are behaviourally equivalent in
DX\longrightarrow\overline{T}DX
.
Moreover, while in the first case the behavioural distance of two states is either
0
or
\infty
(the states are behaviourally equivalent or not), in the second case the enrichment typically gives rise to non-trivial behavioural distances.

We first review in Section
4.1
what in relation to
[
60
]
could be called the “formal theory of endofunctors”.
Relevant references for this section include
[
23
,
60
]
in case of algebras for
{\mathscr{V}}
-enriched monads and
[
35
,
48
]
in case of (co)algebras for (
{\mathscr{V}}
-enriched) endofunctors.

Section
4.2
carries out an analysis of categories of coalgebras for both liftings and extensions, in case of “change-of-base” for the quantale
{\mathscr{V}}
,
extending results of
[
7
,
8
]
. As corollaries we get information about final coalgebras of
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-endofunctors
\overline{T}
as in the diagrams above.

4.1.
A 2-categorical approach to coalgebras

In order to relate coalgebras for different type functors on different categories, it will be convenient to work in a (2-)category where the objects are types (endofunctors) and the arrows are certain natural transformations that allow to connect types on different base categories.
For the basic notions of
2
-categories that we need in this section we refer to
[
42
]
.

{defi}

Let
{\mathscr{C}}
be a 2-category.
The category
\mathsf{Endo}({\mathscr{C}})
has objects
(X,T)
given by 1-cells
T:X\longrightarrow X
in
{\mathscr{C}}
.
An arrow
(F,\varphi):(X,T)\longrightarrow(X^{\prime},T^{\prime})
is given by a 1-cell
F:X\longrightarrow X^{\prime}
and a 2-cell
\varphi:FT\longrightarrow T^{\prime}F
.
A 2-cell
\sigma:(F,\varphi)\longrightarrow(F^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime})
is a 2-cell
\sigma:F\longrightarrow F^{\prime}
such that
T^{\prime}\sigma\circ\varphi=\varphi^{\prime}\circ\sigma T
.

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{\swarrow\varphi}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{\sigma\swarrow}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{\swarrow\varphi}
\scriptstyle{=}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{F^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\scriptstyle{F^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\swarrow\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\swarrow\varphi}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X^{\prime}}

Notice that there is a canonical inclusion 2-functor
\mathsf{Incl}:{\mathscr{C}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Endo}({\mathscr{C}})
mapping each object
X
to the pair
(X,{\mathsf{Id}})
.

The above definition is set-up precisely for the next proposition, which allows us to say, in analogy to the formal theory of monads
[
60
]
, that a general 2-category
{\mathscr{C}}
admits coalgebras (for endo-1-cells) if the inclusion
\mathsf{Incl}:{\mathscr{C}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Endo}({\mathscr{C}})
has a right 2-adjoint.
22
22

22

Notice that changing the orientation of the 2-cells in Definition
4.1
would lead to a different 2-category of endo-1-cells, such that the right 2-adjoint to the inclusion (if exists) produces instead the “category of algebras” for the endofunctor
T
.
This result has been independently mentioned by several authors at different moments of time (see for example
[
48
]
for the 2-categorical story, but also the early
[
19
]
which deals with algebras instead of coalgebras):

Proposition 39
.

Let
{\mathscr{C}}
be a 2-category with inserters.
23
23

23

Inserters are dual to coinserters, see Example
2.5
.
Then the inclusion 2-functor
\mathsf{Incl}
has a right 2-adjoint
\mathsf{Coalg}:\mathsf{Endo}({\mathscr{C}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{C}}
, mapping an object
(X,T)
to the inserter of
{\mathsf{Id}}:X\longrightarrow X
and
T
.

Proof 4.1
.

This follows from the correspondences

(1-cell in
{\mathscr{C}}
)
\overline{F}:Z\longrightarrow\mathsf{Coalg}(X,T)

(2-cell in
{\mathscr{C}}
)
\varphi:F\longrightarrow TF
(where
F:Z\longrightarrow X
is a 1-cell in
{\mathscr{C}}
)

(1-cell in
\mathsf{Endo}({\mathscr{C}})
)
(F,\varphi):(Z,{\mathsf{Id}})\longrightarrow(X,T)

In the above, the second bijection is the definition of 1-cells in
\mathsf{Endo}({\mathscr{C}})
, while the first bijection is due to the fact that
\mathsf{Coalg}(X,T)
is an inserter of
{\mathsf{Id}}
and
T
(in that order).
More precisely,

\varphi:F\longrightarrow TF

is an “inserter cone”, and therefore
\varphi
gives rise to
\overline{F}
(that is, to the factorisation through the “inserter cone”).

Remark 40
.

In case
{\mathscr{C}}
is the 2-category of
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-categories,
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functors, and
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformations, recall from

, Sect. 6.1]
how to compute the inserter between two

-functors

:

•

\mathsf{Ins}(F,G)
is the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-category having as objects pairs
(X,\tau)
, where
X
is an object of
{\mathbb{X}}
and
\tau:{\mathbb{1}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Y}}(FX,GX)
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor (which only picks an object, still denoted
\tau:FX\longrightarrow GX
).
The
{\mathscr{V}}
-category-hom
\mathsf{Ins}(F,G)((X,\tau),(X^{\prime},\sigma))
is the equaliser in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
of the parallel pair

\textstyle{{\mathbb{1}}\otimes{\mathbb{X}}(X,X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma\otimes F_{X,X^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Y}}(FX^{\prime},GX^{\prime})\otimes{\mathbb{Y}}(FX,FX^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phantom{MM}c_{FX,FX^{\prime},GX^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{X}}(X,X^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Y}}(FX,GX^{\prime})}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{X}}(X,X^{\prime})\otimes{\mathbb{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G_{X,X^{\prime}}\otimes\tau}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{Y}}(FX,GX^{\prime})\otimes{\mathbb{Y}}(FX,GX)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phantom{MM}c_{FX,GX,GX^{\prime}}}

Explicitly, the objects of
\mathsf{Ins}(F,G)((X,\tau),(X^{\prime},\sigma))
are objects
h:X\longrightarrow X^{\prime}
of the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathbb{X}}(X,X^{\prime})
such that
\sigma\,\cdot\,Fh=Gh\,\cdot\,\tau
, with
{\mathscr{V}}
-distances

\mathsf{Ins}(F,G)((X,\tau),(X^{\prime},\sigma))(h,k)={\mathbb{X}}(X,X^{\prime})(h,k)

for
h,k:X\longrightarrow X^{\prime}
as above.
Composition and identity in
\mathsf{Ins}(F,G)((X,\tau),(X^{\prime},\sigma))
are induced from the composition in
{\mathbb{X}}(X,X^{\prime})
.

•

There is an obvious
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
J:\mathsf{Ins}(F,G)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{X}}
mapping
(X,\tau)
to
X
, and a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation
\iota:FJ\Rightarrow GJ
with components
\iota_{(X,\tau)}:{\mathbb{1}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Y}}(FJ(X,\tau),GJ(X,\tau))
mapping the unique object
0
to
\tau
.

In particular, the above yields the description of the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-category
\mathsf{Coalg}(T)
as
\mathsf{Ins}({\mathsf{Id}},T)
, for any
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
T:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
. More in detail:

•

Objects are
T
-coalgebras, that is, pairs
({\mathscr{X}},c)
where
{\mathscr{X}}
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category and
c:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow T{\mathscr{X}}
is a
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor.

•

The
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
\mathsf{Coalg}(T)(({\mathscr{X}},c),({\mathscr{Y}},d))
is the equaliser of

\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}({\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{Y}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}({\mathscr{X}},d)}
\scriptstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(c,T{\mathscr{Y}})\circ T_{{\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{Y}}}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}({\mathscr{X}},T{\mathscr{Y}})}

that is, it has as objects
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors
f:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
such that
d\circ f=T(f)\circ c
, with
{\mathscr{V}}
-distances
between two such
{\mathscr{V}}
-functors
f,g:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{Y}}
being given by
[{\mathscr{X}},{\mathscr{Y}}](f,g)=\bigwedge_{x}{\mathscr{Y}}(f(x),g(x))
.

•

There is a forgetful
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
\mathsf{Coalg}(T)\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
sending a
T
-coalgebra
({\mathscr{X}},c)
to the underlying
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
.

One of the reasons to introduce the formal category
\mathsf{Endo}({\mathscr{C}})
is that it allows the following characterisation of when adjunctions in
{\mathscr{C}}
can be lifted.

Proposition 41
.

(L,\varphi)\dashv(R,\psi)
in the 2-category
\mathsf{Endo}({\mathscr{C}})
if and only if
L\dashv R
holds in
{\mathscr{C}}
,
\psi
is iso, and
\varphi
is the mate of
\psi^{-1}
under the adjunction
L\dashv R
.

Proof 4.2
.

By standard doctrinal adjunction
[
39
]
.

Since
\mathsf{Coalg}
is a 2-functor, it preserves adjunction of 1-cells, thus we obtain a well-known corollary allowing us to lift an adjunction
L\dashv R
between base objects to an adjunction between “categories of coalgebras”:

Corollary 42
.

Let
{\mathscr{C}}
be a 2-category with inserters, and let
L\dashv R:X^{\prime}\longrightarrow X
an adjunction in
{\mathscr{C}}
.
For any
T:X\longrightarrow X
,
T^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\longrightarrow X^{\prime}
, such that there is an iso-2-cell
\psi:RT^{\prime}\longrightarrow TR
, it follows that:

(1)

R
lifts to
\tilde{R}=\mathsf{Coalg}(R,\psi):\mathsf{Coalg}(X^{\prime},T^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Coalg}(X,T)

(2)

There is an adjunction
\tilde{L}\dashv\tilde{R}:\mathsf{Coalg}(X^{\prime},T^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathsf{Coalg}(X,T)
, where
\tilde{L}
is the image under
\mathsf{Coalg}
of the 1-cell in
\mathsf{Endo}({\mathscr{C}})
given by
L
and the mate of the inverse of
\psi:RT^{\prime}\longrightarrow TR
.

We end this section with observations on the existence and the computation of limits and colimits in
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-categories of coalgebras. Again, these results seem to be folklore, but being unable to find them explicitly in the literature, we provide them below.

Proposition 43
.

The
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-category
\mathsf{Coalg}(T)
, for a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
T:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
, is cocomplete, and has all (weighted) limits that
T
preserves.

Proof 4.3
.

Let
W:\mathcal{K}^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
and
\overline{F}:\mathcal{K}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Coalg}(T)
be
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functors, denoting the weight and the diagram of shape
\mathcal{K}
, a small
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-category.
By the inserter’s universal property, to give
\overline{F}:\mathcal{K}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Coalg}(T)
is the same as to give a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
F:\mathcal{K}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
, together with a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-natural transformation
\xi:F\longrightarrow TF
. Then the colimit
W*F
becomes a
T
-coalgebra with structure
\alpha:W*F\longrightarrow T(W*F)
being the
{\mathscr{V}}
-functor which corresponds to the identity on
W*F
under the composite

\displaystyle{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(W*F,W*F)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle[\mathcal{K}^{\mathsf{op}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}](W,{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(F-,W*F))

\displaystyle\overset{T}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle[\mathcal{K}^{\mathsf{op}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}](W,{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(TF-,T(W*F)))

\displaystyle\overset{\xi}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle[\mathcal{K}^{\mathsf{op}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}](W,{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(F-,T(W*F)))

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(W*F,T(W*F))

Now, let
({\mathscr{X}},c:{\mathscr{X}}\longrightarrow T{\mathscr{X}})
be an arbitrary
T
-coalgebra and consider the diagram below:

\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(T)((W*F,\alpha),({\mathscr{X}},c))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[\mathcal{K}^{\mathsf{op}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}](W,\mathsf{Coalg}(T)(\overline{F}-,({\mathscr{X}},c)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(W*F,{\mathscr{X}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{[\mathcal{K}^{\mathsf{op}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}](W,{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(F-,{\mathscr{X}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(W*F,T{\mathscr{X}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{[\mathcal{K}^{\mathsf{op}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}](W,{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}(F-,T{\mathscr{X}}))}

where the bottom square commutes serially and both columns are equalizers – the left one by construction of
{\mathscr{V}}
-category-homs in the inserter
\mathsf{Coalg}(T)
, and the right one because the representable
[\mathcal{K}^{\mathsf{op}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}](W,-)
preserves limits, in particular equalizers.
Consequently, there is a unique arrow (isomorphism)

\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(T)((W*F,\alpha),({\mathscr{X}},c))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{[\mathcal{K}^{\mathsf{op}},{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}](W,\mathsf{Coalg}(T)(\overline{F}-,({\mathscr{X}},c)))}

exhibiting
(W*F,\alpha)
as the
W
-weighted colimit of
\overline{F}
.

A similar computation that we leave to the reader shows that
\mathsf{Coalg}(T)
has all weighted limits that
T
preserves.

4.2.
Relating behaviours across different base categories

We consider “change of base” across different
{\mathscr{V}}
.
We therefore now write
D^{\mathscr{V}}:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
and
V^{\mathscr{V}}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}_{o}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}_{o}
to distinguish the discrete and forgetful functors for different
{\mathscr{V}}
.
We drop the superscript
(-)^{\mathscr{V}}
in case
{\mathscr{V}}={\mathbb{2}}
.

In the previous section, we have shown that every
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
has a left Kan extension along
D^{\mathscr{V}}
, denoted
H^{\sharp}
.
Now, each such functor induces an ordinary set-endofunctor simply by forgetting the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-structure

\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}_{o}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{H_{o}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}_{o}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V^{\mathscr{V}}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}_{o}}

Moreover,
H^{\sharp}
is a lifting of
V^{\mathscr{V}}H_{o}
according to Proposition
35
.

In the special case when
H
is
D^{\mathscr{V}}T
, the above composite gives back
T
(formally, it is
T_{o}
), and
H^{\sharp}
is
T_{\mathscr{V}}
, the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of
T
.

We will see how the corresponding behaviours are related.
In particular, we will show at the end of the section that
H^{\sharp}
and
V^{\mathscr{V}}H_{o}
, hence
T_{\mathscr{V}}
and
T
, induce the same behavioural equivalence (bisimilarity), while
H^{\sharp}
may additionally induce a behavioural pseudometric.

Remark 44
.

For each commutative quantale
{\mathscr{V}}
, the inclusion (quantale morphism)
\mathsf{d}:{\mathbb{2}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}
given by
0\mapsto\bot,\ 1\mapsto e
, has a right adjoint (as it preserves suprema), denoted
\mathsf{v}:{\mathscr{V}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{2}}
, which maps an element
r
of
{\mathscr{V}}
to
1
if
e\leq r
, and to
0
otherwise.
24
24

24

Notice that
\mathsf{v}
is only a
lax
morphism of quantales, being right adjoint.
This induces as usual the
change-of-base
adjunction (even a 2-adjunction, see
[
24
]
)

\textstyle{{\mathbb{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{d}}
\scriptstyle{\perp}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{v}}
\textstyle{\mapsto}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Preord}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{d}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\perp}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{v}_{*}}

Explicitly, the functor
\mathsf{d}_{*}
maps a preordered set
X
to the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
\mathsf{d}_{*}X
with same set of objects, and
{\mathscr{V}}
-homs given by
\mathsf{d}_{*}X(x^{\prime},x)=e
if
x^{\prime}\leq x
, and
\bot
otherwise.
Its right adjoint transforms a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
into the preorder
\mathsf{v}_{*}{\mathscr{X}}
with same objects again, and order
x^{\prime}\leq x
iff
e\leq{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)
holds.
Hence
\mathsf{d}_{*}X
is the free
{\mathscr{V}}
-category on the preorder
X
, while
\mathsf{v}_{*}{\mathscr{X}}
is the underlying ordinary category (which happens to be a preorder, due to the simple nature of quantales) of the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
. ∎

Note that
\mathsf{d}_{*}
is both a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor and a
{\mathsf{Preord}}
-functor, while its right adjoint
\mathsf{v}_{*}
(in fact, the whole adjunction
\mathsf{d}_{*}\dashv\mathsf{v}_{*}
) is only
{\mathsf{Preord}}
-enriched.

Remark 45
.

If
{\mathscr{V}}
is nontrivial and integral, the embedding
\mathsf{d}:{\mathbb{2}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}
has also a left adjoint
\mathsf{c}:{\mathscr{V}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{2}}
, given by
\mathsf{c}(r)=0
iff
r=\bot
, otherwise
\mathsf{c}(r)=1
.
Notice that
\mathsf{c}
is only a colax morphism of quantales, in the sense that
\mathsf{c}(e)\leq 1
(in fact, here we have equality) and
\mathsf{c}(r\otimes s)\leq\mathsf{c}(r)\land\mathsf{c}(s)
, for all
r,s
in
{\mathscr{V}}
.

We will in the sequel assume that
\mathsf{c}
is actually a morphism of quantales.
The reader can check that this boils down to the requirement that
r\otimes s=\bot
in
{\mathscr{V}}
implies
r=\bot
or
s=\bot
. That is, the quantale has no zero-divisors.
All our examples satisfy this assumption.

If the quantale has no zero-divisors,
\mathsf{d}_{*}
also has a left adjoint
\mathsf{c}_{*}
mapping a
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{X}}
to the preorder
\mathsf{c}_{*}{\mathscr{X}}
with same objects and
x^{\prime}\leq x
in
{\mathsf{c}_{*}{\mathscr{X}}}
iff
{\mathscr{X}}(x^{\prime},x)\neq\bot
. Moreover, the adjunction
\mathsf{c}_{*}\dashv\mathsf{d}_{*}
is
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-enriched:

\textstyle{{\mathbb{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{d}}
\scriptstyle{\perp}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{c}}
\textstyle{\mapsto}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Preord}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{d}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\perp}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{c}_{*}}

From the above remark we obtain the following propositions.

Proposition 46
.

Let
{\mathscr{V}}
be an arbitrary commutative quantale and let
\widehat{T}:{\mathsf{Preord}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Preord}}
be a locally monotone functor (that is,
{\mathsf{Preord}}
-enriched) and
\overline{T}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
be a lifting of
\widehat{T}
to
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
(meaning that
\overline{T}
is a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor such that
\mathsf{v}_{*}\overline{T}\cong\widehat{T}\mathsf{v}_{*}
holds).
Then the locally monotone adjunction
\mathsf{d}_{*}\dashv\mathsf{v}_{*}
lifts to a locally monotone adjunction
\widetilde{\mathsf{d}}_{*}\dashv\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{*}
between the associated
{\mathsf{Preord}}
-categories of coalgebras.

\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(\widehat{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathsf{d}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(\overline{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Preord}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{d}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{v}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{T}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{T}}

Proposition 47
.

Assume that
{\mathscr{V}}
is a non-trivial integral commutative quantale without zero divisors.
Let again
\widehat{T}:{\mathsf{Preord}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Preord}}
be a locally monotone functor, but this time consider
\overline{T}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
be an extension of
\widehat{T}
to
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
(meaning that
\overline{T}
is a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-functor, such that
\overline{T}\mathsf{d}_{*}\cong\widehat{T}\mathsf{d}_{*}
holds).
Then the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-adjunction
\mathsf{c}_{*}\dashv\mathsf{d}_{*}
lifts to a
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-adjunction
\widetilde{\mathsf{c}}_{*}\dashv\widetilde{\mathsf{d}}_{*}
between the associated
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-categories of coalgebras.

\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(\widehat{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathsf{d}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathsf{c}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(\overline{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Preord}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{d}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{c}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{T}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{T}}

We return now to the discrete functor
D^{\mathscr{V}}:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.
It is easy to see that it decomposes as

\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Preord}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{d}_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}}
.
Additionally, recall the following (see
also Example
3.1
(
2
)):

(1)

There are locally monotone functors
D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Preord}}
,
C:{\mathsf{Preord}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
, where
D
maps a set to its discrete preorder and
C
maps a preorder to its set of connected components.

(2)

There is a chain
C_{o}\dashv D_{o}\dashv V:{\mathsf{Preord}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}

of ordinary adjunctions where
V
is the underlying-set forgetful functor.

(3)

The locally monotone adjunction
C\dashv D
is
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-enriched.

The next two propositions from
[
7
]
are similar to the two above, but connect
{\mathsf{Set}}
with
{\mathsf{Preord}}
instead of
{\mathsf{Preord}}
with
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.

Proposition 48
.

Let
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
and
\widehat{T}:{\mathsf{Preord}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Preord}}
an extension of
T
(a locally monotone functor such that
DT\cong\widehat{T}D
). Then the locally monotone adjunction
C\dashv D
lifts to a locally monotone adjunction
\widetilde{C}\dashv\widetilde{D}
between the associated categories of coalgebras:

\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{D}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{C}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(\widehat{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{C}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Preord}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{T}}

Consequently,
\widetilde{D}
will preserve limits, in particular, the final coalgebra (if it exists).

Proposition 49
.

Let
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
and
\widehat{T}:{\mathsf{Preord}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Preord}}
a lifting of
T
(an ordinary functor such that
TV\cong V\widehat{T}
).
Then the ordinary adjunction
D_{o}\dashv V
lifts to an ordinary adjunction
\widetilde{D}_{o}\dashv\widetilde{V}
between the associated categories of coalgebras.

\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{D}_{o}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{V}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(\widehat{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{o}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{V}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Preord}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{T}}

Consequently,
\widetilde{V}
will preserve ordinary limits; in particular, the underlying set of a final
\widehat{T}
-coalgebra will be a final
T
-coalgebra.

Let now
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
and denote by
T_{\mathbb{2}}
is
{\mathbb{2}}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cat}
-ification, that is, its
{\mathsf{Preord}}
-ification
[
8
]
. We plan to see how
T_{\mathbb{2}}
and
T_{\mathscr{V}}
, the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of
T
, are related. We start by the following observation:

Proposition 50
.

The embedding
\mathsf{d}_{\ast}:{\mathsf{Preord}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
is dense.

Proof 4.4
.

We have shown in Theorem
22
that

D^{\mathscr{V}}=\mathsf{d}_{*}D
is
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-dense. Using that

\mathsf{d}_{\ast}
is fully faithful, it follows from
[
40
, Theorem 5.13]
that both
D
and
\mathsf{d}_{*}
are
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-dense and that
\mathsf{d}_{*}=\mathsf{Lan}_{D}(D^{\mathscr{V}})
holds.

Remark 51
.

Let
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
be a set-functor and
T_{\mathbb{2}}
its
{\mathsf{Preord}}
-ification as above. Then the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification
T_{\mathscr{V}}
of
T
can be computed in two stages, as follows:

\displaystyle T_{\mathscr{V}}\

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Lan}_{D^{\mathscr{V}}}(D^{\mathscr{V}}T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathsf{Lan}_{(\mathsf{d}_{*}D)}(\mathsf{d}_{*}DT)=\mathsf{Lan}_{\mathsf{d}_{*}}(\mathsf{Lan}_{D}(\mathsf{d}_{*}DT))

(by
[
40
, Theorem 4.47]
,

because
\mathsf{d}_{\ast}
is fully faithful)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathsf{Lan}_{\mathsf{d}_{*}}(\mathsf{Lan}_{D}(\mathsf{d}_{*}T_{\mathbb{2}}D))

(because
DT\cong T_{\mathbb{2}}D
)

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathsf{Lan}_{\mathsf{d}_{*}}(\mathsf{d}_{*}T_{\mathbb{2}})

where the last isomorphism holds because
\mathsf{Lan}_{D}(\mathsf{d}_{*}T_{\mathbb{2}}D)\cong\mathsf{d}_{*}T_{\mathbb{2}}
. To verify the latter isomorphism, notice first that the
{\mathsf{Preord}}
-enriched left Kan extension
\mathsf{Lan}_{D}(\mathsf{d}_{*}T_{\mathbb{2}}D)
is also the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-enriched left Kan extension of
\mathsf{d}_{*}T_{\mathbb{2}}D
along
D
, by a change-of-base argument as in
[
66
, Theorem 1.7.1]
. Next, apply
[
40
, Theorem 5.29]
to the composite
{\mathsf{Preord}}
-functor
\mathsf{d}_{*}T_{\mathbb{2}}
, using the density presentation of
D
exhibited in
[
8
]
and the fact that
\mathsf{d}_{*}
is
{\mathsf{Preord}}
-left adjoint.

The remark above says that
the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification of an endofunctor
T
of
{\mathsf{Set}}
can be obtained by taking first the
{\mathsf{Preord}}
-ification
T_{\mathbb{2}}:{\mathsf{Preord}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Preord}}
and then the left Kan extension of

\mathsf{d}_{\ast}T_{\mathbb{2}}

along
\mathsf{d}_{*}
,
as in

\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{\mathscr{V}}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Preord}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{\mathbb{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{d}_{\ast}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Preord}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{d}_{\ast}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T}
\scriptstyle{D}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D}

Putting things together we now obtain

Theorem 52
.

Let
{\mathscr{V}}
be a non-trivial integral commutative quantale without zero divisors, and
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
an arbitrary endofunctor, with
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification
T_{\mathscr{V}}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.
Then the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-adjunctions
C\dashv D:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Preord}}
,
\mathsf{c}_{*}\dashv\mathsf{d}_{*}:{\mathsf{Preord}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
lift to
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-adjunctions between the associated
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-categories of coalgebras:

\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{D}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{C}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(T_{\mathbb{2}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathsf{d}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathsf{c}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(T_{\mathscr{V}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{C}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Preord}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{d}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{c}_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{T_{\mathscr{V}}}

Since the
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-ification
T_{\mathscr{V}}
of an endofunctor
T
on
{\mathsf{Set}}
is supposed to be “
T
in the world of
{\mathscr{V}}
-categories”, the theorem above confirms the expectation that final
T_{\mathscr{V}}
-coalgebras have a discrete metric. In fact, we can say that the final
T
-coalgebra is the final
T_{\mathscr{V}}
-coalgebra, if we consider
\mathsf{Coalg}(T)
as a full (enriched-reflective) subcategory of
\mathsf{Coalg}(T_{\mathscr{V}})
.

The next theorem deals with a more general situation where the final metric-coalgebra is the final set-coalgebra with an additional metric. This includes in particular the case where
\overline{T}
is
H^{\sharp}
for some
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
with
V^{\mathscr{V}}H_{o}=T_{o}
.

Theorem 53
.

Let
{\mathscr{V}}
be a commutative quantale,
T:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
be an arbitrary endofunctor,
\widehat{T}:{\mathsf{Preord}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Preord}}
a lifting of
T
to
{\mathsf{Preord}}
, and
\overline{T}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
be a lifting of
\widehat{T}
to
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
.
Then the ordinary adjunction
D_{o}\dashv V:{\mathsf{Preord}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Set}}
and the
{\mathsf{Preord}}
-adjunction
\mathsf{d}_{*}\dashv\mathsf{v}_{*}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathsf{Preord}}
lift to adjunctions between the associated
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-categories of coalgebras

\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(T)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{V}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{D}_{o}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(\widehat{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathsf{v}}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\mathsf{d}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{Coalg}(\overline{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Set}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{V}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{D_{o}}
\scriptstyle{T}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{Preord}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{v}_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{d}_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{T}}

It follows that
\overline{T}
and
T
induce the same notion of behavioural equivalence (bisimilarity). Nevertheless, the final
\overline{T}
-coalgebra can provide additional information about order and metric of non-bisimilar elements.

{exa}

Recall from Example
3.7
(
2
) the functor
TX=X^{A}\times B
and its lifting
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}={\mathscr{X}}^{A}\otimes{\mathscr{B}}
.
Assume that the quantale is integral.
Then the final
H^{\sharp}
-coalgebra is the power
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{B}}^{A^{*}}
having as objects all functions mapping each finite sequence of inputs to the last observable output in
{\mathscr{B}}
, with
{\mathscr{V}}
-distances

{\mathscr{B}}^{A^{*}}(h,k)=\bigwedge_{\mathsf{l}\in A^{*}}{\mathscr{B}}(h(\mathsf{l}),k(\mathsf{l}))

for each pair of behaviour functions
h,k:A^{*}\longrightarrow B
.

In particular, for the lifting
H^{\sharp}
of the stream functor from Example
3.7
(
2a
) we obtain the final
H^{\sharp}
-coalgebra as the
{\mathscr{V}}
-category
{\mathscr{B}}^{\mathbb{N}}
of streams over
B
.
The lifting
H^{\sharp}
of the deterministic automata functor from Example
3.7
(
2b
) given by
H^{\sharp}{\mathscr{X}}={\mathscr{X}}^{A}\otimes{\mathbb{2}}_{r,s}
has now as final coalgebra the “generalised metric space”
{\mathbb{2}}_{r,s}^{A^{*}}
of languages over the alphabet
A
.

5.
Conclusion

This paper is part of a larger endeavour extending set-based coalgebra to
{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
-based coalgebra, see for example
[
65
,
43
,
14
,
8
,
45
,
6
,
20
]
.
Here, we showed that every functor
H:{\mathsf{Set}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
has a left-Kan extension
H^{\sharp}:{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}\longrightarrow{\mathscr{V}}\hskip-1.5pt\mbox{-}\hskip 0.9pt{\mathsf{cat}}
, and that the final
H^{\sharp}
-coalgebra is the corresponding final coalgebra over
{\mathsf{Set}}
equipped with a
{\mathscr{V}}
-metric.

There are several directions in which to expand our results.
For example, it would be interesting to move from commutative to general quantales and to quantaloids
[
53
,
62
]
.
There is also the question whether one can extend not only functors but also monads in a uniform way, which could be related to (metric) trace (bi)simulation as in
[
10
]
.

Coalgebraically, it would be interesting to further develop the topic, barely touched upon in the last section, of behavioural pseudo-metrics
[
31
,
21
,
55
,
70
]
, while on the logical side, we aim to combine this paper with
[
45
,
6
]
in the pursuit of an equational approach to quantitative reasoning about coalgebras/transition systems, an objective related to recent work on quantitative algebraic reasoning
[
49
]
.
